  Budgets apply to live admission before the irreversible commit fence. They are
  deliberately not reapplied to a durable v1 commit manifest during configured
  epoch recovery: an operator may lower the current budget, but a result already
  admitted under the previous run must remain replayable. The limits bound
  Python result metadata and per-parent staged bytes; they do not impose a
  campaign-wide corpus quota, preempt a blocking filesystem call, limit target
  process memory, or replace storage capacity monitoring.

  F343 applies the same pair before `--simulate` creates its five synthetic
  outputs. It proves `5 <= MAX_OBJECTS` and `5 * input_size <= MAX_BYTES`
  before creating the output directory, instead of writing an already-known
  oversized result and rejecting it during discovery. Synthetic mutation then
  opens one no-follow regular source inode, records its device/inode/size/
  mtime/ctime identity, preserves the former 1-to-3-byte `randint` sequence,
  and streams 1 MiB chunks to at most 32 temporary output descriptors per
  batch. Only the currently patched chunk is copied; partial `os.write` calls
  are completed without a second whole-file buffer. After fd/path identity
  revalidation, same-directory hard links publish `sim_NNNN` without
  overwriting an existing or racing destination. Any write, identity, or
  publication failure removes all temporary names and rolls back names
  published by that call. These files are worker-private and intentionally not
  fsync-durable; F340 performs the subsequent durable staged admission. F343
  changes simulation resource/failure semantics, not its random strategy, and
  makes no solver, coverage, campaign, bug-discovery, or LAVA-M claim.

- SYMCC_STANDALONE_INPUT_MAX_BYTES=N (default 268435456, range
  1..1099511627776): F341 hard byte budget for each external seed, recovered
  public corpus object, and worker-private input copy. Rank 0 parses this value
  with the F340 result pair and broadcasts one admission contract before shared
  state is created. `SYMCC_STANDALONE_RESULT_MAX_BYTES` must be less than or
  equal to this input limit, which guarantees that every individually generated
  child admitted by the result-total budget can later be processed as an input.
  Invalid values or an inconsistent result/input pair abort startup with code
  68. The input limit is live policy: lowering it can intentionally reject an
  oversized pre-commit or already-public object, but it does not undo an
  irreversible durable result commit before replay completes.

  External input hashing opens the final component with `O_NOFOLLOW`, verifies
  a regular same-descriptor inode, and consumes fixed 1 MiB chunks. Device,
  inode, size, mtime, and ctime are compared before/after the read and against a
  final no-follow path stat. The rendezvous owner then reopens exactly that
  identity and streams it into an fsync-backed content-addressed publication;
  path replacement or in-place mutation is retried on a later scan instead of
  permanently remembering a partial name. The per-name cache stores this
  identity rather than only the filename, so an atomically replaced AFL queue
  entry is admitted as new content.

  Workers copy public corpus inputs to their private execution path through the
  same stable stream while computing the expected SHA-256. This replaces the
  former `copy2` plus second full-file digest pass. Explicit byte overflow is a
  non-retryable `input-budget-exceeded` control failure and takes the existing
  bounded Abort(70) path with pre-commit state left recoverable. Shared-corpus
  scans use context-managed `scandir`; final statistics count only canonical
  64-hex no-follow regular entries. F342 projects the complete shutdown-time
  external-content observation set onto that same public namespace: present
  external is `public intersection observed-external`, and new interesting is
  `public minus present-external`. Metadata files, symlinks, later external
  queue imports, and an external snapshot that disappeared before owner
  publication therefore cannot inflate or suppress the reported new corpus.

  The limit bounds Python buffer memory and per-object admitted bytes, not the
  number of external filenames, total corpus storage, synchronous syscall
  latency, or target memory. Every master still hashes each new external input
  to compute content-based rendezvous ownership, and the owner performs a
  second stable source pass for atomic publication; F341 does not claim O(1)
  campaign metadata, multi-host throughput, coverage, solver, or bug-finding
  improvement.

- SYMCC_SHARED_STATE_FS_PROBE=0/1 (default 1) and
  SYMCC_SHARED_STATE_FS_PROBE_TIMEOUT=SECONDS (default 5, range 0.001..60):
  Before standalone work ownership, multi-master work/target leases, or
  coverage-owner gossip begins using a distinct shared root, run a fail-fast storage
  capability contract. F328 selects the exact named requirement profile for
  each path role instead of applying one universal operation set. Standalone
  work-state/corpus publication uses `full-shared-state-v1` and verifies all
  nine operations: file and directory fsync, same-directory, cross-directory
  and actual publication-boundary replace, hard link, durable unlink, and both
  advisory-lock observations. Work/target lease roots use `lease-table-v1`,
  which omits cross-directory/publication replace and hard link. A coverage-only
  root uses `coverage-gossip-v1`, which also omits durable unlink because that
  writer only replaces heartbeat/shard records. While the parent holds an
  exclusive nonblocking flock, a fresh isolated Python subprocess must observe
  contention; after the parent descriptor closes, a second subprocess must
  acquire it. Failure aborts initialization instead of silently disabling
  fencing or durability. Probe artifacts are removed and the parent directory
  is synced before success is returned. The standalone frontend additionally
  moves a probe object across the actual hidden work-state root and public
  corpus root, so a nested mount or `EXDEV` publication boundary fails before
  epoch metadata or symbolic-execution results are published. The hybrid
  frontend enumerates the enabled work-lease, target-lease, and coverage-owner
  roots by canonical realpath, merges the required-operation union when
  protocols share one path, and probes each resulting contract before starting
  its asynchronous query service, object store, schedulers, or
  shared owners. A cached snapshot is reused only when its verified-operation
  set covers the later consumer's required set. A hybrid preflight failure
  first runs bounded STOP/exact-ACK
  worker shutdown and then preserves a nonzero MPI initialization result.

  A successful complete nine-operation probe retains the
  `symcc-shared-filesystem-capabilities-v1` snapshot schema. A partial profile
  uses v2: required operations are `true`; deliberately skipped operations are
  JSON `null` and listed under `unverified_operations`. `null` means not tested,
  not unsupported. Failure of any required operation raises an initialization
  error; the framework never returns a successful snapshot with a false required
  capability or silently weakens the storage protocol.

  The local operation contract reports `same-host-subprocess-v1`: it proves
  cooperation between processes on the current host and catches unsupported
  mount operations. F329 adds a second standalone-only qualification before
  epoch metadata publication. Every master reports the actual identity returned
  by `MPI_Get_processor_name` over a master-only communicator. When at least two
  distinct processors participate, each processor representative holds one
  stable `.cluster-filesystem.lock` inode in turn; every other master must obtain
  EACCES/EAGAIN from a nonblocking exclusive flock, and a representative on a
  different processor must acquire it after descriptor-close release. With H
  processors and M masters, success therefore contains H holder rounds,
  H*(M-1) contention observations, and H remote release observations. F351
  closes the remaining namespace-identity gap before that evidence can be
  committed. Every master first revalidates the current state/publication
  device and filesystem ID against its local capability, keeps an
  `O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW` state-root descriptor and an `O_NOFOLLOW` regular
  lock descriptor across all rounds, then reopens the public root and leaf and
  requires both `(st_dev, st_ino)` identities and the exact token bytes to
  match. All lock opens and preparation after root acquisition are relative to
  that root descriptor. A final master exchange requires exactly M namespace
  identity checks. The upgraded snapshot is therefore v3 and reports
  `probe_scope=cross-host-mpi-lock-v2`, exact membership/round/contention/
  release/identity evidence, and `cluster_lock_verified=true`. The historical
  v1 scope is not accepted as current runtime-renewal evidence.
  F351 adds no environment switch. It strengthens the existing startup and
  renewal gates.

  F352 closes the renewal-result replay and field-splicing boundary.
  It does so without adding an environment switch. After the F351 structure
  gate succeeds, every master computes a domain-separated SHA-256 proof
  transcript over the epoch,
  exact qualification generation, ordered `(rank, processor)` membership,
  processor representatives, and round/contention/release/identity counts.
  Fixed-width big-endian integers and explicit UTF-8 byte lengths make the
  encoding deterministic. A separate `qualification-transcript-commit` phase
  requires all masters to publish the same generation and transcript before
  the result can become verified. The result object carries both fields.

  Runtime completion recomputes that transcript with the controller's current
  in-flight generation, reruns the complete evidence-shape gate, requires the
  ordered result ranks to equal the configured master ranks, and requires the
  result capability to equal the startup capability on that client. Thus a
  previous-generation result, a zero-round or membership splice, and a result
  from another state root all fail closed. Rank 0 prints the accepted startup
  and renewal transcript for audit. The transcript is an unkeyed integrity
  digest for trusted framework components, not a signature or MAC; it does not
  provide Byzantine-master authentication.

  F353 makes the local renewal-result consumer failure-atomic, again without
  adding an environment switch or changing the wire/capability schema. The
  completion gate now requires an exact integer generation and totalizes
  malformed member/representative/result fields as a rejected proof. Elapsed
  telemetry must be an exact finite nonnegative int/float; invalid values reject
  the result and contribute zero time instead of contaminating later metrics
  with NaN.

  Every operation that can reject or raise now precedes the controller's single
  state-commit block: F351/F352 proof admission, next-generation jitter
  derivation, elapsed accumulation, and overflow checking. A recognized bad
  proof completes the generation as one failure, preserving
  `attempts == successes + failures`. An unexpected validator, scheduler, or
  arithmetic exception leaves generation, in-flight typestate, scheduling, and
  all metrics exactly at their pre-call values so the fatal control path reports
  a coherent diagnostic snapshot. The existing
  `symcc-cluster-lock-renewal-metrics-v2` schema is unchanged.

  F354 closes the caller-side exception boundary around that failure-atomic
  completion, without adding a switch or changing any MPI, capability, proof,
  metrics, or disk schema. Runtime code calls
  `complete_cluster_lock_renewal()` rather than enumerating selected exception
  classes around the controller method. A valid proof returns `(true, "")`; a
  recognized proof rejection returns `(false, "")` after committing one
  failure; any ordinary Python `Exception` returns `(false, bounded_error)`
  with the exact F353 pre-state retained. The error is normalized to at most
  512 characters and enters the existing fatal-control shutdown path.
  `BaseException` process-control signals such as `KeyboardInterrupt` and
  `SystemExit` are deliberately not swallowed. This is a rank-local exception
  containment boundary, not communicator repair, rank replacement, ULFM, or
  fault-tolerant continuation.

  F355 closes the producer-side exception boundary around the public cluster
  lock qualification API, also without adding a switch or changing MPI,
  capability, proof, metrics, or disk schemas. The protocol implementation is
  private; the public `qualify_mpi_cluster_advisory_lock()` calls it once and
  returns successful results unchanged. Any ordinary Python `Exception` now
  becomes a structurally valid fail-closed result with `clean=false`, zero
  proof counters, an empty transcript, a valid input generation when available,
  and a sanitized total diagnostic of at most 512 characters. Invalid
  capability and generation inputs are normalized, and `elapsed=0.0` denotes
  unavailable trustworthy timing for the escaped internal failure.

  Startup therefore reaches its existing `Abort(65)` gate, while runtime
  renewal passes the failed result through F353/F354 admission and into the
  existing fatal-control shutdown. `BaseException` process-control signals are
  deliberately not swallowed. This totalizes rank-local result production; it
  does not broadcast exceptions, repair communicators, replace ranks, provide
  ULFM recovery, or eliminate peer waits before the existing bounded deadline.

  F356 moves that boundary one stage earlier, around the rank-local inputs used
  by cluster-lock qualification. It adds no environment switch and changes no
  MPI tag, capability, proof, metrics, or disk schema. Startup filesystem
  probing and startup/runtime processor observation now run through
  `observe_cluster_lock_qualification_inputs()`. Any ordinary Python
  `Exception` becomes a bounded local error while the rank still enters the
  existing membership exchange; capability and processor probes are each
  called at most once, and a filesystem-probe failure does not skip processor
  observation. Invalid return values are normalized to `None` and the empty
  processor identity. `BaseException` process-control signals still propagate.

  Membership records now require a nonempty processor exactly when
  `local_probe_ok=true`. An explicit failed record may carry an empty processor
  so peers preserve its actual observation error and stop before representative
  selection or lock rounds. Rank, field set, types, processor length, and NUL
  checks remain strict. Thus the change makes negative evidence expressible; it
  does not enlarge the successful proof domain. Runtime lease heartbeat and
  the clocks before/after qualification remain separate failure boundaries.

  F357 closes those two runtime-renewal boundaries and the adjacent work-lease
  heartbeat adapter. It adds no environment switch and changes no MPI tag,
  capability, proof, or disk schema. `observe_work_lease_heartbeat()` calls a
  provider at most once, accepts only a duplicate-free tuple of canonical work
  hashes, and maps every ordinary `Exception` or malformed return to a bounded
  error. The coordinator validates the complete renewed/lost partition before
  changing local heartbeat metrics or tokens. A failure can still follow
  partially completed filesystem side effects, so production fails closed and
  deliberately does not retry an uncertain heartbeat.

  Runtime qualification reuses the event-loop `now` for
  `last_lease_heartbeat` instead of taking another pre-protocol clock sample.
  `complete_cluster_lock_renewal()` now owns the optional completion-clock
  call, so a clock `Exception` becomes an accounting error while the F353
  controller pre-state remains unchanged. `BaseException` signals propagate.
  This is rank-local containment plus collective negative evidence, not
  external-I/O rollback, real MPI fault tolerance, or ULFM recovery.

  F358 closes the ordinary-exception boundary around the adjacent renewal
  request transport. It adds no environment switch and changes no MPI tag,
  request schema, proof, capability, metric, or disk format. Root-side
  `begin_cluster_lock_renewal()` now distinguishes failures before generation
  creation from failures after `begin_request()`: a post-begin failure returns
  the nonzero generation and every successfully created nonblocking send
  handle, so the caller can still enter the bounded qualification required by
  peers that received the request. Peer polling similarly contains ordinary
  communicator, receive, and request-admission failures. A safely received but
  malformed request remains quarantinable; a local admission exception enters
  fatal-control instead.

  `observe_cluster_lock_renewal_delivery()` inspects each unique send handle at
  most once and reports completed, incomplete, and uncertain counts. A false
  `Test()` result is not conflated with an exception while observing `Test()`;
  either non-success state fails closed without automatically retrying an
  operation whose external completion is uncertain. Diagnostics remain bounded
  to 512 characters and `BaseException` process-control signals propagate.
  This preserves partial transport state; it does not make MPI send atomic,
  roll back delivered messages, repair a communicator, or provide ULFM.

  F359 separates the repository's Python test-discovery environments. It adds
  no runtime environment switch and does not change compilation, solver, MPI,
  coverage, or corpus behavior. The root `pytest.ini` sets `testpaths = test`,
  so an unqualified `python3 -m pytest` discovers the SymCC-Parallel project
  tests without entering the separately built vendored QSYM test environment.
  No `norecursedirs` or `--ignore` rule hides QSYM: explicitly naming its test
  path remains supported after following the QSYM README prerequisites. The
  authoritative commands and lifecycle distinction are documented in
  `docs/Testing.txt` and the F359 research report.

  Multiple masters on one processor record their membership but retain
  `same-host-subprocess-v1`, zero cluster rounds, and
  `cluster_lock_verified=false`; configuration cannot override processor
  identity. The hybrid frontend normally has one master per MPI job and still
  has no honest cross-job control plane, so its snapshots remain local-scope.
  Even a successful standalone cross-host lock litmus proves only the currently
  participating clients' advisory-lock domain. It does not prove data-cache
  coherence, server/lock-manager failover, network-partition behavior, kernel
  panic, power-loss durability, concurrent jobs reusing one epoch, or
  non-cooperating writers. Filesystem type/source, device/fsid, and distributed
  classification remain diagnostic and never upgrade proof scope by themselves.
  Set the probe to 0 only for a controlled compatibility experiment; later real
  operations continue to fail closed.

  F330 turns a successful standalone cross-host startup result into a renewable
  runtime qualification lease instead of treating it as permanent. Rank 0 is
  the only process allowed to initiate a renewal on the retained master-only
  communicator. Its request carries the work epoch, the exact next generation,
  and a SHA-256 domain-separated control token. Peers accept only generation
  n+1; malformed, replayed, skipped, or token-mismatched requests cannot enter
  the lock protocol. The qualification exchange token is also generation-bound,
  while the stable lock-file identity remains epoch-bound.

  Before entering each renewal, every master group-commits its current work
  lease heartbeats. The runtime qualification deadline is capped at one third
  of `SYMCC_STANDALONE_WORK_LEASE_TTL`, so the health check itself cannot consume
  the entire lease-validity window. Rank 0 does not start a renewal while a
  global quiescence probe/commit is prepared or when a configured wall-time
  deadline has no more than the effective probe timeout plus one second left.
  A successful renewal replaces the in-memory capability with the latest
  membership evidence. Missing participants, transport errors, loss of a
  second processor, or failed exclusion/release evidence become a global work
  control failure followed by bounded worker shutdown and nonzero MPI abort;
  the old startup snapshot is never used as a fallback. Renewal completion
  additionally requires v2 scope, a current-generation F352 transcript, the
  complete F351 evidence shape, exact result/capability proof fields, the live
  ordered master ranks, and the client-local startup capability. Standalone
  runtime setup also requires the identity count to equal the live master
  count; a legacy capability or stale result cannot bypass requalification by
  retaining only a true boolean flag.

  Runtime renewal is enabled only when startup produced
  `cluster_lock_verified=true`, v2 proof scope, and complete identity evidence.
  Same-processor standalone runs, single-master
  runs, disabled filesystem probing, and the hybrid cross-job topology do not
  retain the communicator and cannot claim runtime cross-host monitoring. The
  check bounds stale-evidence exposure to approximately interval plus protocol
  timeout while all masters remain scheduled; it does not prove storage-server
  failover correctness, tolerate failed MPI ranks, or replace ULFM-style
  communicator repair.

- SYMCC_SHARED_STATE_CLUSTER_PROBE_TIMEOUT=SECONDS (default 30, range
  0.001..3600): One monotonic deadline shared by standalone MPI membership,
  stable-record preparation, all holder/contention/release/identity exchanges,
  the F352 transcript-commit phase, and send completion. Normal exchanges poll
  after 0.5 ms and exponentially back off to 10 ms when no progress occurs.
  Missing, malformed, stale, contradictory, or late evidence causes `Abort(65)`
  before `state.json`, lease records, or symbolic-execution results are
  published. The setting has no effect when `SYMCC_SHARED_STATE_FS_PROBE=0` or
  in the hybrid frontend.

- SYMCC_SHARED_STATE_CLUSTER_REQUALIFY_INTERVAL=SECONDS (default 60, range
  0..86400): Period between successful standalone runtime cross-host lock
  qualifications before bounded jitter is applied. The interval is measured
  from completion, so probes do not overlap or accumulate after a slow round.
  `0` disables runtime renewal and frees the master-only qualification
  communicator after startup. Positive values have an effect only after a real
  multi-processor startup litmus has set `cluster_lock_verified=true`. Runtime
  probes use the timeout configured by
  `SYMCC_SHARED_STATE_CLUSTER_PROBE_TIMEOUT`, capped at one third of the current
  standalone work-lease TTL, and are suppressed near quiescence and wall-time
  shutdown boundaries. Each master emits
  `symcc-cluster-lock-renewal-metrics-v2` with generation, attempts, successes,
  failures, measured protocol time, jitter fraction and adjacent scheduled
  intervals on both normal and semantic-failure exit paths. Successful root
  completion also prints the generation-bound F352 proof transcript. F353
  guarantees that every committed attempt contributes exactly one success or
  failure; an internal pre-commit exception contributes neither and preserves
  the in-flight state. F354 converts every ordinary completion exception into
  a bounded accounting error so the runtime reaches that fatal-control
  shutdown path instead of unwinding one rank with an uncaught exception.

- SYMCC_SHARED_STATE_CLUSTER_REQUALIFY_JITTER=FRACTION (default 0.1, range
  0..0.5): Deterministic delay-only jitter for standalone runtime lock
  qualification. Rank 0 derives each generation's delay from a domain-separated
  SHA-256 of the job epoch and generation, producing a reproducible interval in
  `[interval, interval * (1 + fraction))`; peers only follow the authenticated
  generation request and never start from local timers. Different jobs and
  generations therefore avoid a shared fixed cadence without global RNG or
  fork-correlated state. Because jitter never shortens the base interval, it
  cannot increase the configured probe frequency. The conservative stale-proof
  window becomes `(1 + fraction) * interval + effective probe timeout + master
  scheduling delay`; set `0` for the F330 fixed cadence. Jitter does not add
  retries or weaken the existing fail-closed response.

  Before any runtime generation, all qualified masters perform a bounded
  point-to-point configuration agreement. The committed record contains the
  epoch and the exact big-endian binary64 encodings of the effective interval,
  probe timeout, and jitter fraction; signed zero is normalized. Every master
  must observe the same SHA-256 fingerprint within 5 seconds. Runtime request
  schema v2 binds that fingerprint again, and a post-consensus field mutation
  is rejected both before transmission and again before a qualification result
  can advance generation, metrics, or the next cached schedule. A missing rank,
  malformed record, or any
  interval/timeout/jitter drift leaves generation and attempts at zero, shuts
  down local workers, performs a bounded master-only cleanup rendezvous, and
  exits fail closed with code 70. This agreement detects MPI launch-environment
  drift; it is not ULFM communicator repair and does not survive a failed rank.
  The renewal controller is consensus-gated by default: until the exact local
  fingerprint is sealed by the bounded agreement, `due()` remains false and
  root request creation, peer acceptance, and result completion all fail
  closed. The code-only opt-out exists for isolated pre-F332 protocol tests and
  is not used by the production MPI frontend.

  After live agreement succeeds, the standalone frontend also binds the exact
  configuration to `<output>/.standalone-work-<epoch>/renewal-configuration.json`
  using canonical JSON, file fsync, hard-link create-if-absent, directory fsync,
  a 4096-byte regular-file bound, and exact-byte reread. An existing exact
  record takes a read-only fast path; a different, malformed, oversized, or
  symlinked record fails closed before generation 1 without overwriting the
  prior commitment. This F333 manifest fences recovery of an interrupted work
  epoch. After a clean persistent-output completion, F335 moves it with the
  work-state into a reserved retired root; a later bounded GC removes that
  root. It is therefore not a permanent experiment-results database. A
  pre-F333 legacy epoch with no record establishes its first commitment on the
  first successful post-upgrade startup.

  Clean standalone completion uses a two-barrier retirement protocol. After
  worker ACKs and the master-only cleanup rendezvous, every rank first enters a
  bounded pre-cleanup global barrier. Before completing the active name, the target
  must be a direct child of the shared root and its basename must match exactly
  `.standalone-work-<64 lowercase hexadecimal digits>`; an ordinary corpus
  directory, truncated epoch, or differently cased name fails closed without
  mutation. For a persistent output root, rank 0 first requires Linux
  `renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)`: ordinary check-then-rename is not an accepted
  fallback. The kernel atomically renames the tree to
  `.retired-standalone-work-<epoch>-<32 lowercase hexadecimal digits>` and
  rejects an existing destination with EEXIST while preserving both names. On
  success the frontend fsyncs the shared root. This removes recursive deletion
  from the collective completion path while keeping active-name disappearance
  durable. A program-
  owned temporary output root is still removed completely. Non-root ranks wait
  in a second bounded barrier and cannot enter `MPI_Finalize` until rank 0
  completes that barrier. A rename, removal, or parent-directory fsync failure
  aborts with code 72;
  a pre-cleanup or post-cleanup barrier timeout aborts with code 71 or 73,
  respectively. If the rename or removal became visible but its directory fsync
  failed, persistence across a storage crash is unknown and the run deliberately
  does not report success. The F334 gate protects completion semantics; F335 changes
  its persistent-output completion action from recursive deletion to durable
  name retirement, but does not make storage operations transactional or
  provide a distributed consensus log.

  Before starting a service on a persistent output root, rank 0 qualifies that
  exact mount in a private, random, process-owned probe directory. One
  no-replace rename must succeed, a second rename to the existing destination
  must fail with EEXIST, and durable probe cleanup must succeed. A missing libc
  symbol, unsupported filesystem flag, silently clobbering implementation,
  create/fsync failure, or cleanup uncertainty is broadcast to all ranks and
  aborts before service with code 67. The probe directory is never removed
  unless this process successfully created it. This F336 gate adds one fixed
  persistent-startup cost; it is not part of worker constraint solving. The
  local overlayfs mechanism benchmark measured an 11.944 ms median and 13.024
  ms P95 over 60 probes, while the no-replace completion primitive added 5.068
  us at the median relative to durable replace (1.002x) over 60+60 interleaved
  samples. These are local mechanism costs, not DSE throughput or coverage
  results. Non-Linux and unsupported filesystems deliberately fail closed.

- SYMCC_RETIRED_WORK_STATE_GC_LIMIT=0..1000000 (default 1): Maximum number of
  exact `.retired-standalone-work-<64-hex epoch>-<32-hex retirement id>` roots
  that rank 0 selects for incremental reclamation before starting a standalone
  service on a persistent output directory. Zero disables automatic
  reclamation. Reclaimers serialize through the stable
  `.symcc-retired-work-state-gc.lock`, acquired with a bounded crash-released
  advisory lock and `O_NOFOLLOW`. The complete reserved namespace is
  type-checked before any deletion; a malformed reserved name, symlink,
  non-directory, lock timeout, traversal/removal failure, or directory-fsync
  uncertainty aborts startup with code 66.

  F337 combines the root limit with the entry and cooperative-time budgets
  below. Each selected tree is traversed iteratively through directory file
  descriptors opened with `O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW`; files and symlinks are
  unlinked relative to their parent descriptor, empty directories are removed
  relative to their parent, and surviving dirty directories are fsynced before
  returning. Reaching a budget is a successful partial step: the valid retired
  root remains and a later startup resumes from its remaining entries. An EOF
  followed by `ENOTEMPTY` reopens the same directory iterator because directory
  mutation during `scandir` can make one enumeration incomplete. Startup logs
  report completed roots, removed entries, stop reason, effective budgets, and
  any partial root. The last completed run can remain retired until a later
  startup or maintenance action reclaims it.

  F338 preserves complete reserved-namespace validation but streams the shared
  root instead of retaining every `DirEntry` and candidate. A reverse lexical
  heap holds at most `GC_LIMIT` names and selects exactly the same lexical
  smallest roots as the former full sort, independent of `scandir` order.
  Startup telemetry separately reports scanned entries, valid candidates, and
  selected roots. This bounds retained Python candidate state, not the complete
  `getdents`/type-validation work or one blocking filesystem operation.

  F339 independently hardens worker-result staging and corpus replay. Staging
  directories are consumed through a context-managed streaming `scandir`;
  every normalized object name must belong to the committed result manifest
  before type or content I/O. Content-addressed verification opens the final
  path component with `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK|O_CLOEXEC`, requires the opened
  descriptor to be a regular file through `fstat`, and hashes bytes from that
  same descriptor. A missing `O_NOFOLLOW`, symlink, non-regular object, unknown
  staged name, or I/O uncertainty fails closed. Live results still require the
  exact staged inventory; durable replay may recover expected objects from the
  public corpus, but public objects use the same no-follow digest. This removes
  the additional full `DirEntry` snapshot and prevents unauthorized objects
  from consuming hash I/O; expected/observed manifest sets and complete hashing
  of valid objects remain proportional to the result manifest.

- SYMCC_RETIRED_WORK_STATE_GC_ENTRY_BUDGET=1..10000000 (default 4096): Hard
  upper bound on successful `unlink` and `rmdir` directory-entry mutations
  across all retired roots selected during one startup. Removing the retired
  root itself counts as one mutation. Namespace discovery, type validation,
  open/scandir calls, failed calls, and fsyncs do not consume this count. The
  tree itself is monotonic recovery state; no separate GC cursor is required.

- SYMCC_RETIRED_WORK_STATE_GC_TIME_BUDGET_SECONDS=0.001..3600 (default 0.05):
  Finite cooperative time budget beginning after the GC lock is acquired and
  before reserved-namespace validation. The deadline is checked between
  filesystem operations after at least one successful mutation, so a deep tree
  cannot make zero progress forever. It is not a hard wall-clock bound: one
  blocking `openat`, `getdents`, `unlinkat`, `rmdir`, or `fsync` cannot be
  preempted by this Python loop and may exceed the configured duration. Lock
  acquisition has its own bounded timeout. Complete top-level namespace
  discovery, storage latency, retained disk usage, and long periods with no
  later startup are not bounded by these three settings.

- SYMCC_MULTI_MASTER_LEASES=0/1 (default 0): Enable a shared fenced lease table
  for deployments with multiple MPI masters/coordinators writing to the same
  SymCC output tree. Before dispatch, a master must atomically claim the work id
  and receive a fencing token. Worker results complete the shared lease only if
  that token still matches, so a stale master cannot mark work done after another
  master has stolen an expired pre-commit lease. Once `begin_commit` succeeds,
  the record is deliberately non-stealable; replaying a partially applied batch
  would require a separate durable transaction journal that is not implemented.

- SYMCC_MULTI_MASTER_LEASE_DIR=PATH (default
  "$AFL_OUT/<symcc-name>/.work_lease_table"): Directory for the shared fenced
  lease table. Each work id is stored in a deterministic shard as a small JSON
  record. Schema, record id, payload, token, state, and finite Unix timestamp
  are validated before mutation or recovery. Stable regular-file kernel flocks
  serialize short claim/complete updates, while long-running worker executions
  are protected by fencing tokens rather than filesystem locks.

- SYMCC_MULTI_MASTER_TARGET_LEASES=0/1 (default 1 when
  SYMCC_MULTI_MASTER_LEASES=1), SYMCC_MULTI_MASTER_TARGET_LEASE_DIR=PATH
  (default "$AFL_OUT/<symcc-name>/.target_lease_table"), and
  SYMCC_MULTI_MASTER_TARGET_LEASE_TTL=SECONDS (default
  SYMCC_WORK_LEASE_TTL): Enable a second fenced table keyed by branch target
  rather than exact work payload. A directed replay atomically claims its
  primary branch and every non-skipped S2F action target before scheduler
  attempts/cooldowns are committed. Overlapping groups therefore conflict even
  when different coordinators use different seeds. Pending and active groups
  are heartbeated, stale fencing tokens cannot release a newer owner's group,
  and successful/failed dispatches release rather than permanently complete
  targets so later evidence-guided retries remain possible.

- SYMCC_MULTI_MASTER_LEASE_SHARDS=N (default 256), SYMCC_MASTER_ID=ID (default
  hostname:pid), SYMCC_MULTI_MASTER_LOCK_TTL=SECONDS (default 30, range
  1..3600), and SYMCC_MULTI_MASTER_LOCK_ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT=SECONDS (default 60,
  range 0.001..3600): Tune the shared lease shard count, owner identifier,
  compatibility/diagnostic lock age hint, and monotonic acquisition deadline.
  Work and target record updates use persistent-file kernel flocks; they are
  never time-stolen and are automatically released on process exit. Timeout or
  unsupported filesystem locking fails closed. Active leases are heartbeated
  by the master at roughly one third of SYMCC_WORK_LEASE_TTL, capped at 30s.

- SYMCC_COVERAGE_GOSSIP=0/1 (default 1 when multi-master leases are enabled):
  Use persistent coverage-owner shards for final AFL bitmap novelty decisions.
  Each candidate bit is committed under a short shard lock against one
  authoritative OR-state, making hit-count bucket novelty globally unique even
  when coordinators triage identical candidates concurrently. Coordinators
  periodically pull newer shard epochs into their local bitmap and worker delta
  journals.

- SYMCC_COVERAGE_OWNER_DIR=PATH (default
  "$AFL_OUT/<symcc-name>/.coverage_owner") and
  SYMCC_COVERAGE_OWNER_SHARDS=N (default max(16, SYMCC_BITMAP_SHARDS)):
  Shared location and shard count for authoritative coverage state. The current
  implementation requires atomic same-directory rename, file/directory fsync,
  and cross-process kernel flock semantics. One stable `.lock` file is retained
  per visited shard; it must not be deleted while coordinators are running.

- SYMCC_COORDINATOR_INDEX=N (default 0), SYMCC_COORDINATOR_COUNT=N (default 1),
  and SYMCC_COVERAGE_OWNER_TTL=SECONDS (default 30): Identify coordinators and
  control heartbeat failover. Shard modulo coordinator count names the preferred
  live owner; another coordinator may proxy an OR-commit while holding the
  shard lock, preserving progress when the preferred owner is unavailable.
  Non-finite timestamps and malformed shard records fail closed instead of
  becoming immortal owners or empty authoritative state.

- SYMCC_COVERAGE_OWNER_LOCK_ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT=SECONDS (default 60, range
  0.001..3600): Monotonic deadline for the coverage-owner shard flock. Timeout
  propagates as a control error; no wall-clock age threshold can delete or steal
  a live holder. Process exit releases the kernel lock even when Python cleanup
  and `finally` blocks do not run.

- SYMCC_DPOR=0/1 (default 0): Enable bounded schedule exploration for pthread
  targets in the MPI helper. The master injects util/symcc_schedule_rt.c through
  LD_PRELOAD for worker executions, records synchronization traces, and queues
  logical-thread-id replay prefixes for dependent scheduling points on the same
  mutex/rwlock/condition/join object. This is a fuzzing-oriented bounded-DPOR
  layer: it seeks coverage-diverse interleavings and deliberately bounds depth,
  backtracking window, and pending queue size rather than attempting exhaustive
  model checking. When `SYMCC_DPOR_MEMORY`/`SYMCC_SCHEDULE_MEMORY` are enabled,
  the Python explorer also consumes compiler-emitted `read`/`write` trace rows,
  annotates them with SC vector-clock happens-before and locksets, and can
  generate causal source-set replay prefixes for unprotected memory conflicts.
  Explorer state schema v4 persists bounded sleep sets, ordered wakeup-tree
  leaves, observed Mazurkiewicz-equivalence digests, and ConDPOR-style revisit
  identities. Each schedule artifact contains checked
  `symcc-bounded-source-dpor-v1`, `symcc-bounded-wakeup-tree-v1`, and
  `symcc-bounded-condpor-graph-v1` certificates. This remains a bounded fuzzing
  mode: it does not claim unbounded soundness, completeness, or optimality.

- SYMCC_DPOR_WAKEUP_TREE=0/1 (default 1): Enable ordered bounded wakeup-tree
  insertion. The checker implements the Optimal-DPOR weak-initial recursion
  over deterministic per-thread next events, rejects sleep-set and earlier-leaf
  equivalents, and consumes cooperative runtime-ready snapshots when complete
  evidence exists. This proves only the recorded bounded-tree invariants, not
  whole-program Optimal-DPOR optimality.

- SYMCC_DPOR_CONDPOR_GRAPH=0/1 (default 1): Build the bounded execution graph
  used for ConDPOR-style backward revisits. The graph records stable nodes,
  program order, observed/inferred read-from, per-object coherence, add order,
  causal-successor deletion, deterministic bounded maximal extensions, and
  cross-trace revisit identities. Revisit replay prefixes share the existing
  Source-DPOR queue. Changed read values may alter later control flow, so
  observed suffix restoration is evidence for this bounded trace only and is
  not a complete ConDPOR interpreter.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_PRELOAD=PATH (default auto-detect build/libsymcc_schedule_rt.so):
  Path to the native schedule runtime used when SYMCC_DPOR=1. The runtime
  intercepts pthread_create, mutex lock/trylock/unlock, rwlock rd/wr/unlock,
  condition wait/signal/broadcast, join, detach, and cancellation. It writes
  "seq tid op object"
  rows to SYMCC_SCHEDULE_TRACE and consumes one tid per line from
  SYMCC_SCHEDULE_PREFIX. Created threads receive monotonically allocated logical
  ids. The same logical id is used as the object for `create`,
  `create_success/create_fail`, `thread_start/thread_exit`, join/detach/cancel
  outcomes, and `thread_retire`, avoiding process-address or opaque-pthread-
  handle identity in lifecycle constraints. The fixed 4096-entry mapping table
  reserves a logical identity before create, binds the handle after success,
  releases joinable entries after successful join, and releases detached
  entries only after both detach and exit. `join_cancelled` preserves the target
  mapping so another thread can still join it.

- SYMCC_DPOR_MEMORY=0/1 (default 0 at compile time): Ask the SymCC compiler
  pass to insert `_sym_notify_schedule_read/write` calls around non-atomic,
  non-volatile LLVM loads and stores. This is intentionally separate from
  SYMCC_DPOR because memory tracing can be very high overhead.

- SYMCC_DPOR_ATOMIC_ONLY=0/1 (default 0 at compile time): Insert native
  schedule probes for atomic load/store, atomicrmw, cmpxchg, and fence without
  enabling ordinary load/store or basic-block schedule instrumentation. The
  probe sequence is pre-hook, real atomic instruction, concrete value/result
  evidence when losslessly representable in at most 64 bits, and commit-hook.
  This mode is intended for bounded native concurrency replay with a small,
  auditable event surface.

- SYMCC_DPOR_SCHEDULE_ONLY=0/1 (default 0 at compile time): Build the concrete
  native schedule-probe artifact without regular symbolic shadow-memory
  instrumentation, libc wrapper redirection, or SymCC runtime construction.
  This mode also suppresses LLVM LowerAtomicPass so the target's atomic IR
  remains atomic. Use it together with SYMCC_DPOR_ATOMIC_ONLY=1; the resulting
  binary is the native replay/control-plane artifact, not the input/path
  solving artifact. Keeping these artifacts separate avoids assuming that the
  QSYM global shadow map is safe for concurrent access by target threads.
  Correspondingly, the regular QSYM and simple input/path-solving runtimes bind
  their global symbolic state to the thread that initializes the runtime and
  abort if another target thread reaches an instrumented basic block. This is a
  deliberate fail-closed capability boundary: native schedule exploration must
  use the separate schedule-only artifact; the regular runtime does not claim
  per-thread shadow memory, solver contexts, or concurrent path constraints.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_MEMORY=0/1 (default 0 at runtime): Enable the schedule preload
  runtime to turn compiler-emitted memory notifications into `read`/`write`
  trace rows. `SYMCC_DPOR_MEMORY=1` at runtime is also accepted as a shorthand
  for this switch. Without the preload runtime these notification calls resolve
  to no-op functions in the normal SymCC runtime.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_ATOMIC_COMMIT=0/1 (default 0 at runtime): Enable F425
  two-phase prefix advancement for compiler-emitted atomic probes. A matching
  atomic pre-hook stores thread-local (group,prefix-index) state but does not
  advance the replay prefix. After the real atomic instruction and its value
  notifications, atomic_commit executes a seq_cst fence and advances the
  prefix only when both identities still match. Commit mismatch and nested
  pending-atomic conflict are logged as negative evidence; the native campaign
  rejects either condition. Old binaries and non-atomic hooks retain immediate
  prefix advancement when this option is disabled.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_MEMORY_BYTES=N (default 8, maximum 4096): Maximum number of
  byte-granular objects emitted for one load/store notification. Byte-granular
  rows let the Python HB/lockset analyzer identify overlapping memory
  conflicts while bounding trace growth.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_MEMORY_FILTER=none|stack|owner|shared|dynamic|all (default
  none): Optional provenance filters for compiler-emitted schedule memory
  notifications. `stack` suppresses accesses whose address falls in the current
  thread's pthread stack range. `owner`, `shared`, and `dynamic` enable a
  bounded dynamic ownership table: first same-thread accesses are remembered but
  not logged; when a different logical thread first touches the same byte, the
  runtime marks it shared and emits a compressed prior-access row plus the
  current row when at least one side is a write. `all` combines stack and owner
  filtering. These filters reduce trace volume for experiments; they are not a
  sound alias/provenance analysis.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_MEMORY_STACK=0/1 and SYMCC_SCHEDULE_MEMORY_OWNER=0/1 (default
  0): Explicit aliases for enabling the stack and dynamic-owner filters without
  parsing `SYMCC_SCHEDULE_MEMORY_FILTER`.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_MEMORY_PROVENANCE=0/1 (default 0): Append a compatible fifth
  trace-field tag to memory rows, such as `prov=stack`, `prov=heap`,
  `prov=module`, `prov=anonymous`, `prov=system`, or `prov=unknown`. The stack
  classification uses pthread stack bounds; the remaining classes come from a
  bounded `/proc/self/maps` lookup. `SYMCC_SCHEDULE_MEMORY_FILTER=tag`,
  `tags`, or `provenance` is accepted as a shorthand. Python trace parsing keeps
  these tags in `ScheduleEvent.tags`; schedule artifacts expose
  `provenance_counts`, and joint schedule/query validation propagates the same
  counts for benchmark stratification.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_MEMORY_OWNERS=N (default 65536, maximum 1048576): Number of
  entries in the dynamic owner table. Collisions beyond the bounded probe window
  fall back to logging the access, preserving evidence rather than silently
  dropping potentially shared memory events.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_CONSTRAINT_OUT=PATH (default empty): When SYMCC_DPOR is
  enabled, append one `symcc-schedule-constraint-v1` JSONL artifact per
  observed schedule trace. Each record contains the normalized trace digest,
  target branch, current replay prefix, bounded schedulable-event summary,
  SC/HB conflict summary, the replay prefixes generated from those conflicts,
  and checked Source-DPOR, wakeup-tree, and bounded ConDPOR execution-graph
  certificates. This artifact is for reproducible experiments and later joint
  input/schedule solving; it is not a proof of exhaustive or optimal DPOR.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_SMT_OUT=PATH (default empty): When SYMCC_DPOR is enabled,
  append one `symcc-schedule-smt-v7` JSONL artifact per observed schedule
  trace. The artifact stores one shared SMT-LIB2 QF_LIA base context plus a
  delta for each conflict-derived replay prefix and a ready-to-run incremental
  `push/check-sat/pop` script. `materialize_schedule_smt_query()` can combine
  the base and one delta into a standalone query. Hard constraints encode a
  position permutation, memory-model-aware program order, exact next-event bindings for
  the replay thread prefix, reversal of its source conflict, complete
  mutex/rwlock critical-section nonoverlap, and condition-wait mutex
  release/reacquire lifetimes. Complete thread evidence additionally requires
  create before child start and target exit before successful join return.
  Vector-clock happens-before edges and
  signal/broadcast wake explanations are emitted as optional Boolean
  assumptions because those synchronization edges or spurious wakeups may
  change under replay. F58 additionally puts bounded read-from/coherence
  constraints for tagged memory events into the authoritative base formula
  under the selected SC, TSO, or RA subset. General runtime enabledness,
  unobserved aliases, and untagged concrete values remain outside the formula,
  so SAT means bounded model admissibility rather than concrete schedule
  reachability. The reader accepts v1 through v7 artifacts for backward
  materialization compatibility.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_SMT_MAX_EVENTS=N (default 128, minimum 2, maximum 512):
  Bound the schedulable events represented by each schedule-SMT query. A replay
  candidate whose source-conflict endpoints fall outside this window is not
  emitted as a partial query; the artifact increments `dropped_query_count`
  and sets its truncation flags instead.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_SMT_MAX_QUERIES=N (default 64, minimum 1, maximum 512):
  Bound the conflict-derived SMT query deltas exported for one observed trace.
  Additional source-style replay candidates remain available to the DPOR queue,
  but the SMT artifact increments `dropped_query_limit_count` and sets its
  query truncation flag. The artifact stores one shared base context plus
  bounded per-candidate deltas, so this limit controls evidence volume without
  changing schedule exploration.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_MEMORY_MODEL=SC|TSO|RA (default SC): Select the authoritative
  bounded memory consistency constraints embedded in each schedule-SMT
  artifact. SC uses a global order and last-write read-from. TSO relaxes
  store-to-load global program order while enforcing same-thread store
  forwarding and FIFO stores. RA uses per-location modification-order ranks,
  read-from, program-order/happens-before closure, and release-write to
  acquire-read synchronization for events tagged
  `mo=relaxed|release|acquire|acq_rel|seq_cst`. Non-SC certificates cannot be
  materialized as pthread-only replay prefixes because that runtime does not
  control hardware memory propagation. RA is a research subset, not full
  C11/C++ atomics: fences, release sequences, RMW, mixed-size accesses,
  non-atomic race UB, and the global seq_cst order are not encoded.

  Schedule SMT uses the portable SMT-LIB signed-integer form for read-from
  initial-source sentinels: `(- 1)`, never the Z3-tolerated bare token `-1`.
  The same printer is used by SC/TSO domains, RA coherence, RMW/seq_cst
  constraints and Query-IR read-value bridges. Research evidence runs every
  installed backend and treats parse errors as failures; current executable
  gates agree on SC=UNSAT and TSO/RA=SAT under system libz3 and cvc5 1.1.2.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_SMT_MAX_MEMORY_EVENTS=N (default 32, minimum 0, maximum 64):
  Bound read/write events receiving read-from and coherence variables. Memory
  events outside this cap are recorded as truncation metadata and cannot
  support a full-memory-semantics claim for that trace. Keeping this bound
  separate from `SYMCC_SCHEDULE_SMT_MAX_EVENTS` makes weak-memory formula growth
  explicit in benchmark artifacts.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_SMT_SYNC_STATE=0/1 (default 1): Extend
  `symcc-schedule-smt-v7` with bounded mutex/rwlock, condition-variable, and
  thread lifecycle state. The
  exporter pairs pre-call `lock`/`trylock`/`rdlock`/`wrlock` events with their
  `acquire` or failure outcome and a matching `unlock`/`rwunlock`. Only
  critical sections whose acquire and release both occur inside the bounded
  lifecycle window become hard constraints. Incompatible complete sections on
  the same object must not overlap; two `rdlock` sections may overlap. The
  relative order of controlled lock attempts is linked to schedule `pos_*`
  variables, while lifecycle outcomes and releases use `sync_ord_*` variables.
  A `trylock_fail` busy explanation is emitted as an optional assumption and
  is not asserted by default. Open, unmatched, or truncated lifecycles are
  counted in `sync_state` metadata but do not create hard exclusion constraints,
  avoiding false UNSAT from incomplete evidence.

  `pthread_cond_wait` and `pthread_cond_timedwait` additionally emit
  `wait_mutex_release` before blocking and `wait_mutex_acquire` after a normal
  wake or timeout. The exporter closes the waiter's pre-wait mutex section and
  opens a new reacquired section. A successful `wake` receives an optional
  `cond_wake_signal_*` assumption whose integer witness must select a matching
  `signal` or `broadcast` between release and reacquire. One `signal` witness
  cannot serve two asserted wakes, while a `broadcast` witness may be reused.
  The assumption is not asserted by default, preserving POSIX spurious-wakeup
  behavior; timed-out waits never receive a signal witness. The lifecycle
  model also pairs successful create/start/exit, mapped join/detach/cancel
  outcomes, join cancellation, and explicit identity retirement events.
  A child may start before `pthread_create` returns, but never before the
  pre-call create marker; a join attempt may precede target exit and block, but
  `join_success` must follow `thread_exit`. Create and join failures, unmapped
  handles, missing exits, and truncated lifecycles are counted without adding
  hard completion constraints. A cancelled join does not consume the target or
  merge its exit clock. Complete retirement evidence requires target exit and
  the successful join, successful detach, or detached-create trigger to precede
  `thread_retire`. Vector clocks propagate parent state at `thread_start` and
  child completion only at `join_success`.

  The lifecycle window is five times `SYMCC_SCHEDULE_SMT_MAX_EVENTS`, capped at
  512, to accommodate create/start/exit/join/outcome evidence. Set this option
  to 0 to retain the F45 structural event-order model inside the v7 artifact.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_SMT_ORDER_ENCODING=partial|permutation (default partial):
  Select the ordering representation for `sync_ord_*` lifecycle events. The
  default `partial` mode declares unbounded integer ranks and emits only strict
  program-order, schedule-link, ownership, wake, create, and join edges. It
  omits per-event `0..N-1` bounds and the global `distinct` constraint.
  Since v6 each controlled lifecycle rank is linked by one scaled anchor
  `sync_ord_i = (L+1) * pos_j`, replacing the former quadratic pairwise links.
  The scale leaves at least L integer slots between adjacent controlled events,
  enough to embed every bounded non-controlled lifecycle event while preserving
  strict order. Any satisfying rank model therefore defines an acyclic partial
  order that can be topologically extended to a total lifecycle order. The
  schedulable `pos_*` variables remain a bounded permutation because replay
  slots require exact positions.

  `permutation` preserves the v4-style bounded all-different lifecycle model
  and pairwise links for differential validation and ablation. Artifacts record
  `lifecycle_order_encoding`, `linear_extension_semantics`,
  `order_link_encoding`, semantic-pair/actual-link counts, anchor stride, and
  bound/distinct counts. Unknown values conservatively fall back to `partial`.

  Every current artifact also contains `symcc-lifecycle-order-ir-v1` and a checked
  `symcc-lifecycle-linear-extension-v1` certificate for the observed trace.
  `schedule_linear_extension_certificate()` can consume complete `pos_*` and
  `sync_ord_*` model assignments, optionally recheck a query's replay slots and
  conflict reversal, select satisfied nonoverlap directions, construct a
  deterministic topological order, and project it to controlled logical thread
  IDs. `verify_schedule_linear_extension_certificate()` independently checks
  the event-position permutation and program order, source ranks,
  fixed/selected edges, scaled anchors, total-order preservation, query
  bindings, digest, and replay projection.

  To materialize a runtime prefix:

      python3 util/symcc_schedule_linearize.py schedule-smt.jsonl \
        --model schedule-model.json --query-index 0 \
        --prefix-out schedule.prefix \
        --certificate-out schedule-certificate.json

  The model JSON must contain `event_positions` and `lifecycle_ranks`, either
  as arrays or maps keyed by `pos_i` and `sync_ord_i`. Omitting `--model`
  materializes the embedded observed certificate. Certificates cover the hard
  lifecycle/order IR and the selected query only; optional observed-HB,
  trylock-busy, condition-wake assumptions, path feasibility, and concrete
  runtime reachability remain outside this certificate. A query whose replay
  slots or conflict endpoints include memory read/write events is still
  certifiable as an SMT order, but is marked `runtime_replayable=false`;
  the pthread prefix writer and CLI reject it because the preload runtime
  directly controls pthread scheduling points, not memory accesses.

  v7 stores an authoritative exact `base_smt2` and a hashed
  `relaxed_base_smt2` that omits complete-section nonoverlap choices. The
  relaxed base is internal to the direct solver only: after each SAT check,
  model ranks are checked against every structured choice and violated
  disjunctions are asserted as libz3 ASTs before re-checking. Only a model with
  no remaining violation can produce a certificate. External materialization
  and `incremental_smt2` continue to use the exact base.

  To solve directly with system libz3 and avoid text-model conversion:

      python3 util/symcc_schedule_solve.py schedule-smt.jsonl \
        --query-index 0 --result-out schedule-result.json \
        --certificate-out schedule-certificate.json \
        --prefix-out schedule.prefix

  Use `--eager` for the exact-base ablation, `--base-only` for the shared
  context, and `--max-refinement-rounds` to bound refinement. A
  `refinement_limit` result is explicitly non-exact and never carries a
  certificate.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_QUERY_VALIDATION_OUT=PATH (default empty): When the MPI helper
  launches `symcc_query_service.py` and `SYMCC_SCHEDULE_CONSTRAINT_OUT` is set,
  ask the query service to consume schedule-constraint rows and append
  `symcc-joint-schedule-query-v1` JSONL records. A record binds schedule trace
  digest/replay prefixes to matching Query IR roots, then conservatively reports
  whether the current witness observed the prefix, whether a solver assignment
  satisfies all Query IR roots, or whether the path side remains unknown/UNSAT.
  This remains the inexpensive replay-validation export path.

  For research runs requiring one solver witness, the programmatic
  `QueryStore.solve_joint_schedule_smt()` API consumes a stored Query IR query
  and a `symcc-schedule-smt-v7` artifact, then solves Query IR QF_BV, schedule
  QF_LIA, and tagged read-from byte bridges in one system-libz3 context. It
  emits `symcc-joint-path-schedule-rf-v1`, reruns the bounded Query IR evaluator,
  and independently rechecks hashes, the schedule certificate, RF selectors,
  and byte bindings. Memory trace tags use `sym-byte=N`, `init=0xNN`, and
  `value=0xNN`. Missing tags are not guessed, and the result remains a bounded
  formula witness rather than a whole-program reachability proof.

- SYMCC_DPOR_MAX_DEPTH=N (default 64), SYMCC_DPOR_WINDOW=N (default 32),
  SYMCC_DPOR_PREFIXES=N (default 256), and SYMCC_DPOR_PENDING=N (default
  4096): Bound replay-prefix length, dependent-pair scan window, per-input
  generated prefixes, and the persistent pending queue under
  "$AFL_OUT/<symcc-name>/.dpor_schedule.json".

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_WAIT_MS=N (default 100 in the preload runtime): Maximum time a
  worker thread waits for the prefix-selected logical thread before falling back
  and continuing. This prevents replay prefixes from deadlocking when the
  selected thread is not enabled under the current input/path.

- SYMCC_SCHEDULE_ENABLED=0/1 (MPI worker default 1),
  SYMCC_SCHEDULE_ENABLED_SETTLE_US=N (default 1000, maximum 1000000), and
  SYMCC_SCHEDULE_ENABLED_MAX_THREADS=N (default 32, maximum 64): Record
  cooperative ready snapshots for controlled pthread scheduling points. A
  thread registers before entering the replay gate; the gate may wait for the
  bounded settle interval so concurrently arriving threads appear in the same
  snapshot. `complete=1` means complete relative to this bounded registry at
  that decision, not that arbitrary application threads are operationally
  enabled. Setting `SYMCC_SCHEDULE_ENABLED=0` disables these extra rows.

- Content-addressed live-state API: `util.distributed_state.LiveStateStore`
  stores versioned SHA-256 expression nodes, parent-linked solver frames,
  symbolic stores, fixed-size concrete/symbolic pages, and memory roots.
  `fork_memory()` performs page-granular copy-on-write and
  `put_continuation()` returns a checkpoint id equal to the CAS object digest.
  Inspect or compare persisted objects with:

      python3 util/symcc_live_state.py STATE_STORE inspect CHECKPOINT_ID
      python3 util/symcc_live_state.py STATE_STORE memory-diff ROOT_A ROOT_B

  Pass `--page-size N` before the subcommand when a non-default page size was
  used. The CLI verifies every digest/reference and deliberately reports
  `native_resume_supported=false`: this format does not restore a native
  instruction pointer, registers, call stack, or external resources.

- Path-dependent schedule events (enabled with the existing
  `SYMCC_DPOR_MEMORY=1` compiler instrumentation): conditional branches and
  switches emit `constraint` rows, and instrumented basic-block entries emit
  `action` rows. The preload runtime links a constraint to the last schedule
  read and records its byte width. ConDPOR backward revisit withholds a
  read-dependent constraint and its control-flow suffix for regeneration rather
  than restoring an observed suffix whose events may no longer exist.

- Operational enabledness artifacts: cooperative `ready` rows include bounded
  `offers=tid:operation:object:auxiliary` records. The Python explorer restores
  mutex/rwlock/join/wait state and embeds a
  `symcc-operational-enabledness-v1` certificate in wakeup, ConDPOR, and
  schedule-constraint artifacts. Status is three-valued
  (`enabled|blocked|unknown`); unsupported mutex types or truncated lifecycle
  evidence stay unknown. A terminal witness additionally requires an explicit
  runtime stop with no pending offers.

- Native atomic schedule tracing: the compiler instruments atomic load/store,
  `atomicrmw`, `cmpxchg`, and fence before LLVM's LowerAtomic pass. Trace tags
  preserve width, success/failure memory order, RMW operation, group identity,
  and `atomic=0|1`. Under `SYMCC_SCHEDULE_MEMORY_MODEL=RA`, the bounded model
  includes RMW immediate-predecessor atomicity, release sequences,
  release/acquire fences, seq_cst ranks, explicit non-atomic race rejection,
  and interval-aware mixed-size read-from. This remains a bounded research
  model, not a complete ISO C/C++ executable semantics.

- Native ConDPOR graph and campaign CLI: `util/symcc_native_condpor.py explore`
  runs one fresh target process per bounded replay prefix, strictly checks
  fallback/commit/prefix evidence, regenerates path-dependent events, and
  admits successors through the authoritative SC/TSO/RA schedule-SMT model.
  `verify` recomputes trace protocol state, relation-graph summaries, SMT
  analyses, parent/query topology, and fixed-point/truncation counters without
  rerunning the external target. Memory-graph bounds are 2..512 modeled events,
  0..64 memory events, and 1..65536 relation candidates. The certificate is a
  bounded mechanism witness, not an unbounded ConDPOR or full ISO C11 proof.

- SYMCC_LIVE_PROGRAM=PATH (default empty): Enable executable continuation-IR
  state work in the MPI helper. PATH must contain a `symcc-live-program-v1`
  object with functions, blocks, and bounded CPS-like instructions. The master
  creates one CAS checkpoint per input seed; workers restore, execute, fork, and
  return resumable frontier checkpoints through the normal fenced state leases.

- SYMCC_LIVE_LLVM=PATH (default empty): If `SYMCC_LIVE_PROGRAM` is unset,
  lower one `.ll`, `.bc`, C, or C++ module to continuation IR in the MPI master
  and use the resulting program for live-state work. An explicitly configured
  `SYMCC_LIVE_PROGRAM` takes precedence and is never overwritten.

- SYMCC_LIVE_ENTRY=NAME (default `main`): Entry function for LLVM continuation
  lowering. Integer entry arguments up to 64 bits consume symbolic input bytes
  consecutively in little-endian order. An entry whose exact signature is
  `(address-space-0 pointer, integer size)` instead uses the bounded symbolic
  input-buffer ABI: the pointer names a dedicated memory object and the size is
  the concrete byte length of the current seed.

- SYMCC_LIVE_PLUGIN=PATH (default auto-discovered `build/libsymcc.so`) and
  SYMCC_LIVE_COMPILER_ARGS=STRING (default empty): Compiler pass and additional
  shell-quoted Clang arguments used by MPI `SYMCC_LIVE_LLVM` lowering.

- SYMCC_LIVE_PROGRAM_OUT=PATH (compiler pass, default unset): Run the
  `live-continuation-export` module pass and write either a
  `symcc-live-program-v1` executable artifact or a
  `symcc-llvm-continuation-lowering-v1` rejection report. This is normally set
  by `llvm_to_continuation.py`.

- SYMCC_LIVE_STRICT=0|1 (default 0): Turn an LLVM lowering rejection into a
  compiler fatal error. Without strict mode the rejection report remains
  inspectable and ordinary compilation may continue.

- SYMCC_LIVE_FEASIBILITY=0|1 (default 1): Before committing a symbolic
  branch/assume state, lower its CAS path-condition frames to QF_BV and query
  the configured solver. UNSAT states are pruned. Unknown, timeout,
  unavailable solver, or unsupported expression conservatively keeps the
  state and increments `feasibility_unknown`.

- SYMCC_LIVE_SOLVER_TIMEOUT_MS=N (default 1000, range 1..3600000):
  Per-feasibility-query timeout. `SYMCC_QUERY_SOLVER` selects the helper;
  otherwise the executor searches PATH and the local build tree.

- SYMCC_LIVE_INCREMENTAL_SOLVER=0|1 (default 1): Keep one
  `symcc-query-solver --server` process per live worker and key its QF_BV
  contexts by the immutable CAS solver-frame root. Two alternatives from the
  same state reuse the exact context through Z3 push/pop. When a child root is
  absent but its parent root is cached, the helper copies the parent's asserted
  formulas and parses only the new frame delta. A cold or evicted context is
  reconstructed from the complete CAS path. Server/protocol failure opens a
  per-executor circuit breaker and falls back to one-shot `--generic`; solver
  UNKNOWN remains conservative. The cache is disposable and is never part of
  checkpoint identity or correctness.

- SYMCC_LIVE_INCREMENTAL_CONTEXTS=N (default 128, range 1..65536):
  Maximum QF_BV prefix contexts retained by each live worker's solver helper.
  The helper uses bounded FIFO eviction. The MPI worker retains one
  `LiveContinuationExecutor` across continuation leases so exact and
  parent-root contexts can survive normal scheduling boundaries.

- SYMCC_LIVE_INCREMENTAL_ARTIFACTS=N (default
  `max(512,4*contexts)`, range 16..262144): Maximum temporary prefix, delta,
  and target SMT fragments retained by one executor. CAS frame metadata and
  normalized SMT fragments use related bounded LRUs. All temporary files are
  removed when the executor closes.

- SYMCC_LIVE_SEARCH=LIST (default `bfs`): Comma-separated interleaving of at
  most eight live continuation search strategies. Supported values are `bfs`,
  `dfs`, `random-state`, `random-path`, `nurs:covnew`, `nurs:md2u`,
  `nurs:depth`, `nurs:icnt`, `nurs:qc`, `subpath`, `target-distance`,
  `loop-exit`, `multi-objective`, `path-cover`, `cbc`, and `cgs`.
  `multi-objective`
  deterministically
  combines location novelty, distance to uncovered code, optional target
  distance, loop exit, path depth, query cost, and interpreter-observed outcome
  feedback. It changes scheduling only and never authorizes SAT, UNSAT, state
  merging, or coverage admission. `path-cover` builds function-local CFG SCC
  condensation DAGs, enumerates a bounded family of minimum path covers, and
  ranks ready checkpoints by compatible-cover support and the uncovered suffix
  remaining from their current component. Its input is the stable branch token
  sequence already stored in each checkpoint. Unknown tokens, ambiguous 64-bit
  token collisions, calls belonging to another function, and edges absent from
  the retained cover family remove no candidates. If the current function is
  unavailable or above its analysis bound, selection falls back to BFS.
  `cbc` enables Compatible Branch Coverage analysis and fork-time pruning.
  It derives function-local data/control dependency closures and greedily
  groups pairwise independent branches. Once state pressure reaches the CBC
  threshold, a prospective state whose latest outcomes disagree within one
  compatible group is not checkpointed. CBC uses FIFO for its own selection
  turn and can be interleaved with the other strategies. Memory-derived,
  cyclic, interprocedural, unknown-assumption, ambiguous-token, and over-budget
  cases fail open and retain states.
  `cgs` enables Concrete Constraint Guided Scheduling. It recognizes an exact,
  function-local subset of concrete `store -> load -> icmp` dependencies,
  including identity chains and one `and`/`or` mask. After only one outcome of
  an admitted concrete branch has been observed, ready states whose latest
  concrete store value satisfies the uncovered outcome receive highest FIFO
  priority. Because one continuation claim may run through several
  instructions, a state currently at a relevant store is admitted to that FIFO
  only when a conservative RHS lookahead already yields a concrete satisfying
  value.
  CGS never prunes a state or decides feasibility. Symbolic addresses/values,
  guarded or overlapping stores, memory-affecting calls, ambiguous definitions,
  token/site collisions, and over-budget functions fail open to ordinary FIFO.

- SYMCC_LIVE_SEARCH_SEED=N (default 1, unsigned 64-bit) and
  SYMCC_LIVE_SUBPATH_LENGTH=N (default 2, range 1..8): Configure the versioned
  SplitMix64 stream and subpath counter used by live-state search. A persistent
  frontier binds these values at initialization; changing either while work is
  active fails closed instead of silently changing restart order.

  Search snapshot v2 added bounded per-context integer statistics:
  attempt/completion counts, live-interpreter location gain, executed steps,
  solver queries, and failed completions. Claim records exactly one attempt;
  completion rebases observations onto the latest frontier generation and then
  records at most one completion. Thus two workers discovering the same
  location cannot both receive novelty credit. Version-1 snapshots remain
  readable and are upgraded with empty feedback. Context capacity reserves an
  `@overflow` bucket so a full table remains serializable and restorable.

  Search snapshot v3 additionally binds the two live path-cover construction
  bounds below. Version-1 and version-2 snapshots restore with defaults and are
  rewritten using the current schema. Search snapshot v4 additionally binds
  the three CBC controls below; version-1 through version-3 snapshots restore
  them with defaults. Search snapshot v5 additionally binds the four CGS
  controls and persists instruction count, branch-outcome masks, partial-target
  order, observation count, and dropped-target count. Version-1 through
  version-4 snapshots restore CGS defaults with empty observations. This
  prevents workers with different environments from silently rebuilding
  different analysis families or rotating different target windows for one
  persistent frontier.

- SYMCC_LIVE_MPC_COVERS=N (default 8, range 1..256): Maximum number of
  distinct minimum path covers retained per live function. Enumeration uses
  deterministic maximum bipartite matching plus a bounded matched-edge
  exclusion budget. It searches for useful alternatives but does not claim to
  enumerate every maximum matching.

- SYMCC_LIVE_MPC_FUNCTION_NODES=N (default 4096, range 16..262144): Maximum
  number of basic blocks in one function admitted to live path-cover
  construction. Larger functions retain the ordinary live search behavior.
  Cover construction is skipped entirely unless `path-cover` appears in
  `SYMCC_LIVE_SEARCH`.

  Both `resume()` and `resume-persistent` expose a derived
  `state_search.path_cover_graph` object. It reports whether planning was
  enabled, total/admitted/oversized functions, retained SCC components and
  covers, mapped decision tokens, and ambiguous decision tokens. These fields
  diagnose an experiment's actual planning surface; they are not correctness
  state and are intentionally absent from the persistent search snapshot.

- SYMCC_LIVE_CBC_STATE_THRESHOLD=N (default 5, range 1..100000): Minimum
  prospective live-state pressure before CBC may prune. Local execution counts
  its queue, frontier, and new children. Persistent execution also includes the
  durable ready/lease pressure outside the current claim, so a one-state local
  slice still reacts to global frontier growth.

- SYMCC_LIVE_CBC_FUNCTION_NODES=N (default 4096, range 16..262144): Maximum
  basic blocks in one function admitted to CBC post-dominator and dependency
  analysis. Larger functions retain every state.

- SYMCC_LIVE_CBC_BRANCHES=N (default 4096, range 2..65536): Maximum branch and
  `throw_if` sites admitted in one function. Exceeding the limit fails open.
  CBC is built only when `cbc` appears in `SYMCC_LIVE_SEARCH`.

  Execution results expose `state_search.cbc_graph` with function/branch/token
  admission facts and `state_search.cbc_execution` with checks, pruned states,
  fail-open states, threshold activations, and pressure peak. These derived
  fields support audit and experiment stratification; they are not solver
  proofs, coverage bitmaps, or persistent correctness state.

- SYMCC_LIVE_CGS_TARGETS=N (default 10, range 1..1024): Maximum number of
  partially covered concrete branches in the active CGS target window.

- SYMCC_LIVE_CGS_ROTATION_INSTRUCTIONS=N (default 1000000, range
  1..2147483647): Interpreted-instruction interval between deterministic target
  window rotations. Completion rebases instruction and branch events onto the
  latest lease-fenced frontier generation.

- SYMCC_LIVE_CGS_FUNCTION_NODES=N (default 4096, range 16..262144): Maximum
  basic blocks in one function admitted to CGS dependency analysis.

- SYMCC_LIVE_CGS_BRANCHES=N (default 4096, range 1..65536): Maximum concrete
  branch sites admitted in one function and maximum globally retained branch
  outcome records. New sites beyond the retained record budget are counted and
  ignored. CGS analysis is built only when `cgs` appears in
  `SYMCC_LIVE_SEARCH`.

  Execution results expose `state_search.cgs_graph` with static admission and
  fail-open facts and `state_search.cgs_execution` with instruction/branch
  observations, concrete store updates, symbolic invalidations, event batches,
  and active-target count. These are diagnostic scheduling facts, not proof of
  coverage gain, SAT, or solver acceleration.

- Live execution results expose `feasibility_mode`,
  `incremental_context_exact_hits`, `incremental_context_parent_hits`,
  `incremental_context_rebuilds`, `incremental_context_entries`,
  `incremental_server_starts`, `incremental_server_failures`,
  `feasibility_oneshot_checks`, and SMT/frame-cache counters. These are
  per-`resume()` deltas even though the underlying worker cache persists.

- SYMCC_LIVE_MEMORY_LIMIT=N (compiler lowering default 1048576 bytes, range
  64..4194304): Maximum synthetic static-memory image produced by LLVM
  continuation lowering. Only referenced, initialized, address-space 0,
  non-TLS globals enter the image. Exceeding the bound produces a structured
  lowering rejection rather than a truncated object.

- SYMCC_LIVE_INPUT_BUFFER_LIMIT=N (compiler lowering default 65536 bytes,
  range 1..4194304): Maximum reserved capacity of an automatically recognized
  `(pointer,size)` entry input object. The effective capacity is also clamped
  by `SYMCC_LIVE_MEMORY_LIMIT` and the unsigned range of the LLVM size
  parameter. A concrete seed larger than the resulting capacity is rejected;
  reserved capacity never authorizes access beyond the current seed length.

- SYMCC_LIVE_HEAP_OBJECT_LIMIT=N (compiler lowering default 65536 bytes,
  range 1..4194304): Maximum size accepted for one direct
  `malloc(constant-size)` instance. Every accepted site receives a fixed
  synthetic slot pool and remains subject to `SYMCC_LIVE_MEMORY_LIMIT`. The
  modeled allocation is bounded-infallible; allocation-failure paths are not
  explored.

- SYMCC_LIVE_DYNAMIC_HEAP_LIMIT=N (compiler lowering default 4096 bytes,
  range 1..SYMCC_LIVE_HEAP_OBJECT_LIMIT): Physical capacity reserved for each
  slot of a dynamic-size `malloc` or `calloc` site. The requested symbolic
  size is stored separately as the object's logical bound. Zero, arithmetic
  overflow, a request above this limit, or a full site pool returns null in
  the continuation IR. Dereferences constrain both conditional liveness and
  the logical requested size; reserved backing bytes do not enlarge the
  target-visible object.

- SYMCC_LIVE_HEAP_SITE_CAPACITY=N (compiler lowering default 4, range 1..64):
  Number of simultaneously live synthetic instances reserved for each direct
  allocator call site. Fixed-size `malloc` retains the legacy infallible
  contract and reports pool exhaustion as a model-bound error. Dynamic
  `malloc`/`calloc` uses a nullable contract: a symbolic first-free selector
  returns a stable slot or null, and live/size/init expressions survive
  checkpoint migration. Every reserved slot consumes continuation
  memory-image capacity.

- SYMCC_LIVE_ALIAS_LIMIT=N (compiler lowering default 16, range 1..256):
  Maximum number of candidate addresses emitted for one symbolic memory
  operation. For a pointer PHI/select union this is the total across every
  object case, not a per-case allowance. The compiler accepts one integer
  variable offset term with a fixed nonzero scale in each provenance arm.
  Empty or larger alias sets produce a structured rejection; the address is
  never silently reduced to its concrete witness.

- SYMCC_LIVE_STATE_STORE=PATH (default
  `$AFL_OUT/<symcc-name>/.live_states`): Shared content-addressed object store
  used by executable continuation workers. The current MPI implementation
  requires every participating rank to see the same store. CAS replication for
  a non-shared filesystem is not implemented.

- SYMCC_LIVE_PAGE_SIZE=N (default 4096, minimum 64): Page size for continuation
  concrete/symbolic copy-on-write memory. A store must be reopened with the same
  page size used to create its roots.

- SYMCC_LIVE_STEPS_PER_LEASE=N (default 1000, minimum 1) and
  SYMCC_LIVE_STATES_PER_LEASE=N (default 1, minimum 1): Bound instructions and
  in-lease state expansion before resumable checkpoints return to the MPI
  frontier. Only frontier checkpoints count as generated scheduler work;
  states that halt within the lease do not inflate reward.

- SYMCC_MAX_TRANSPORT_INPUT=N (existing default 16 MiB): Also bounds a seed used
  to create an initial live continuation. Oversized live seeds are rejected
