  133.26 seconds, and 471/471 Python tests in 79.599 seconds. The suites were
  run sequentially.

See sota_hybrid_execution_2026.md for the feedback model and evaluation plan.

- F405 certified finite heap-lifetime pointer unions:

  This feature has no environment switch. Continuation lowering enables it
  automatically when the operand of ``free`` has a finite ``select``/PHI
  points-to domain consisting only of already laid-out ordinary heap object
  bases and an optional clean null alternative. The emitted instruction has
  exactly ``op``, ``address``, ``addresses``, and ``bits`` fields, and the
  lowering metadata declares ``bounded-heap-lifetime-pointer-union``.

  ``addresses`` is sorted and unique, contains at most 256 bases, and every
  base must belong to a non-exception heap object. Dynamic indices, nonzero
  object offsets, stack/global pointers, exception-arena objects, pointer
  widths above 64 bits, unknown provenance, or a mixed supported/unsupported
  domain reject continuation lowering. The implementation does not silently
  truncate a domain or round an interior pointer to its owner base.

  At execution time the address expression width must equal the certified
  width. The executor constrains the address to null or one currently live
  certified object, then conditionally clears that object's live, dynamic-size,
  and byte-initialization markers in one state. This avoids an alias-choice
  continuation fork; it does not implement POSE initial symbolic-heap
  materialization, arbitrary pointer arithmetic, or a general alias-aware
  MemorySSA proof after the release. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Certified_Finite_Heap_Lifetime_Union_F405_2026-08-15.md``.

- F406 collective dominating heap-union initialization:

  This feature adds no ``SYMCC_*`` switch. It is enabled only when continuation
  lowering proves that at least two ordinary local heap alternatives are
  collectively covered by at least two different scalar stores that dominate
  the load. Each contributing store must have a single finite pointer
  alternative, sufficient width, no dynamic index, and an interval that covers
  the candidate load. The existing single-store proof runs first, so historical
  accepted programs and artifacts are not reclassified.

  Successful loads carry sorted unique ``initialization_bases`` and the
  ``bounded-collective-heap-union-initialization`` capability. The consumer
  requires heap-lifetime and pointer-union dependencies, reconstructs every
  alias address's owning object at the load width, and requires exact equality
  with the certificate. Runtime ``live`` and byte-initialization markers remain
  authoritative. Partial covers continue to fail closed; branch-local stores
  are handed to F407 only after this proof fails. Dynamic indices, stack/heap
  mixtures, interprocedural summaries, and exception arenas remain outside the
  F406 support domain. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Collective_Heap_Union_Initialization_F406_2026-08-15.md``.

- F407 guard-correlated heap-union initialization:

  This feature adds no ``SYMCC_*`` switch. After the historical single-store
  proof and F406 collective dominance proof fail, continuation lowering may
  admit an acyclic branch/jump region whose merge has 2 through 64 predecessor
  paths. The nearest common dominator is the guard-tree root; each path is
  limited to 64 blocks and eight conditional decisions. A select condition or
  merge pointer-PHI edge discriminator must select one ordinary capacity-1 heap
  object per path, and a singleton non-atomic/non-volatile store on that path
  must cover the load's static byte interval.

  Successful loads retain F406 ``initialization_bases`` and add an
  ``initialization_guard_tree`` with schema
  ``symcc-guarded-heap-union-initialization-v1``. The lowering metadata declares
  ``bounded-guard-correlated-heap-union-initialization``. The consumer
  independently enumerates the artifact CFG, validates branch/PHI guards,
  ordinary-heap owners, mandatory allocation-identity guards, and the exact
  store block/ordinal/address/width. Conflicting repeated conditions, cycles,
  missing paths, unknown live guards, dynamic indices, loops, and unsupported
  calls fail closed.

  The certificate does not set runtime initialization markers. This is a
  bounded MemoryPhi-inspired proof, not LLVM MemorySSA/AA clobber serialization
  or complete POSE. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Guard_Correlated_Heap_Union_Initialization_F407_2026-08-16.md``.

- F408 LLVM MemorySSA/AA bounded heap initialization:

  This feature adds no ``SYMCC_*`` switch. Under the new pass manager, live
  continuation export first promotes scalar allocas, invalidates affected
  function analyses, and then acquires fresh ``AAManager`` and
  ``MemorySSAAnalysis`` results. After the historical single-store, F406, and
  F407 proofs fail, a heap load may be admitted by walking its real LLVM
  ``MemoryUse -> MemoryPhi/MemoryDef`` defining graph.

  Every MemoryPhi incoming must terminate at a simple singleton-pointer store
  covering the load interval. An intervening store is skipped only when its
  allocation/static interval is disjoint or LLVM AA returns ``NoAlias``. A
  non-store definition is currently limited to a directly lowered internal
  ``CallInst`` and is skipped only when ModRef has no ``Mod`` bit. Unknown
  definitions, liveOnEntry, cycles, dynamic offsets, partial stores,
  May/Partial/MustAlias, and graph-limit violations fail closed.

  Successful loads contain ``initialization_memoryssa`` with schema
  ``symcc-memoryssa-aa-heap-initialization-v1`` and declare
  ``bounded-memoryssa-aa-heap-initialization``. Limits are 128 nodes, 64
  incoming edges per MemoryPhi, depth 8, and 64 skipped definitions per
  terminal. The consumer reconstructs logical CFG predecessors, heap ownership,
  pointer-PHI edge correlation, store identity, and direct-call identity. LLVM
  remains responsible for AA/ModRef facts; runtime remains responsible for real
  live/size/byte-init state. See
  ``codex/research-progress/MemorySSA_AA_Heap_Initialization_F408_2026-08-16.md``.

- F409 callsite-instantiated interprocedural heap effect summary:

  This feature adds no ``SYMCC_*`` switch. After the local F408 proof fails, a
  heap load may use ``bounded-interprocedural-heap-effect-summary`` only when
  its real caller MemoryUse reaches a directly lowered internal CallInst. The
  admitted callee is one basic block with one return and is either a unique
  malloc/calloc wrapper or a void argument initializer with one simple scalar
  store at ``formal_parameter + constant_offset``.

  Successful loads contain ``initialization_interprocedural`` with schema
  ``symcc-interprocedural-heap-effect-summary-v1``. The certificate binds the
  caller callsite, callee, actual/formal parameter or returned allocation,
  store and return operation identity, exact load/store interval, and every
  skipped definition. A finite returned heap pool carries one v1 certificate
  per object; the consumer requires exact equality with load, allocator, and
  store alias domains. The current production bounds are 64 skipped definitions,
  256 returned alternatives, and a generated 64-callsite conformance fixture. Recursion, indirect calls,
  invoke/EH, conditional or multiple writers, dynamic intervals, and unknown
  memory effects fail closed.

  The consumer cannot recompute LLVM alias analysis; it closes artifact
  identity and structure while LLVM remains responsible for NoAlias/NoModRef
  facts. The certificate never sets runtime initialization state: live object,
  logical size, and byte-init markers remain authoritative. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Interprocedural_Heap_Effect_Summary_F409_2026-08-16.md``.

- F410 bounded symbolic-length region effect and dynamic byte-lane cover:

  This feature adds no ``SYMCC_*`` switch. LLVM intrinsics and external C
  declarations for ``memset``, ``memcpy``, and ``memmove`` may use a symbolic
  length when source/destination provenance yields one static object interval
  of at most 64 bytes. Lowering emits an unsigned capacity assume when needed,
  one ``offset < length`` guard per byte, and guarded byte reads/writes. Copy
  and move read all source bytes before the first destination write.

  A later dynamic-index scalar load may declare
  ``bounded-symbolic-length-byte-lane-cover`` when its real MemoryUse reaches
  the unique region writer and every finite address/lane pair lies within the
  writer interval. Successful loads carry
  ``initialization_dynamic_byte_lane`` with schema
  ``symcc-symbolic-length-byte-lane-cover-v1``. Current hard bounds are 64
  region bytes, 256 load aliases, 2,048 lane witnesses, and 64 skipped scalar
  definitions. One stack object or one guarded ordinary-heap alternative is
  supported. MemoryPhi, multiple region effects, dynamic writer bases,
  unknown calls, partial cover, volatile/atomic effects, and overlapping
  clobbers fail closed.

  The consumer replays the whole-function region schemas, read/write order,
  CFG dominance, owner, exact index domain, lane Cartesian product, and
  artifact identity. LLVM remains responsible for MemorySSA and AA facts.
  Runtime guarded stores conditionally update byte-init markers, and the
  dynamic load still requires actual per-lane initializedness; the certificate
  does not bypass that check. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Symbolic_Length_Byte_Lane_Cover_F410_2026-08-16.md``.

- F411 loop-carried MemoryPhi byte-lane induction:

  This feature adds no ``SYMCC_*`` switch. A dynamic-index scalar load may
  declare ``bounded-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction`` when its real LLVM
  MemoryUse reaches a canonical loop-header MemoryPhi. The admitted loop has
  one preheader, body, latch, and exit, with the header as the exit's unique
  predecessor; its sole loop PHI induction is seed zero,
  unsigned unit step, and ``ult`` against an input-derived bound whose source
  width can represent the complete writer domain. The MemoryPhi incoming
  definitions must be exactly liveOnEntry and one simple byte store.

  Successful loads carry ``initialization_loop_memoryphi`` with schema
  ``symcc-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction-v1``. It binds the loop and PHI
  edge-copy blocks, recurrence, guard/bound, writer/load alias and index domains,
  object owner, and every load-address/lane to a unique writer iteration.
  Consumer replay requires identity nodes in the input/parameter provenance to
  preserve width and permits at most one strictly widening zext, matching the
  producer's LLVM bound shape.
  The v1 hard bounds are 256 aliases, 2,048 lane witnesses, a one-byte writer,
  and a 1--8 byte scalar load. Multi-latch, nested, conditional,
  extra-PHI, call/alloca, multi-memory-access, partial-cover, atomic/volatile,
  and cross-object loops
  fail closed.

  Runtime byte-init markers remain authoritative for zero-trip and early-exit
  paths; the certificate proves potential cover and never marks a byte initialized.
  If an alias-bearing load/store address simplifies to a concrete value outside
  its declared address domain, the executor still applies the alias constraints
  and makes the state infeasible. An in-domain concrete value keeps the normal
  object lifecycle and logical-size diagnostics. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Loop_MemoryPhi_Byte_Lane_Induction_F411_2026-08-16.md``.

- F412 strided loop MemoryPhi residue-class byte-lane cover:

  This feature adds no ``SYMCC_*`` switch. Generalized F411 loads declare both
  ``bounded-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction`` and
  ``bounded-strided-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction`` and use
  ``symcc-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction-v2``. The producer accepts a
  positive constant induction step in [1, 64], a positive affine writer scale,
  and a 1--8 byte scalar writer when ``writer_bytes <= step * scale``.

  The v2 transcript retains the store's complete alias/index domain and adds
  the exact recurrence-reachable subset, address stride, residue origin,
  covered residues, and a writer-start/writer-lane/iteration witness for every
  load byte. A partial residue set is admitted only when every address in the
  load alias domain is fully covered. Producer and consumer both require the
  bound source to cover the last writer and satisfy
  ``bound_max <= 2^induction_bits - step`` so the recurrence cannot wrap.

  Consumer validation binds the transcript to the actual ``pointer_offset``
  base/index/scale, scalar PHI edge copies, guard, store aliases, object owner,
  and exact lane Cartesian product. Missing capabilities, a capability without
  a v2 use point, overlapping writers, uncovered residues, bool-valued integer
  fields, descending/zero/oversized steps, conditional writers, multiple
  latches or extra memory effects fail closed. Runtime byte-init remains the
  authority for zero-trip and early-exit paths. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Strided_Loop_MemoryPhi_Residue_Cover_F412_2026-08-16.md``.

- F413 conditional loop MemoryPhi guard-carry byte-lane proof:

  This feature adds no ``SYMCC_*`` runtime switch. Conditional writer loops
  declare ``bounded-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction`` and
  ``bounded-conditional-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction`` and use
  ``symcc-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction-v3``. A generalized stride,
  affine scale, or multi-byte writer additionally requires the F412
  ``bounded-strided-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction`` capability.

  The accepted CFG is exactly
  ``header -> decision -> writer|skip -> latch -> header`` with one exit load.
  The writer guard is one integer ``icmp`` defined in ``decision``; the writer
  may be its true or false successor. Header MemoryPhi must merge
  ``liveOnEntry`` with a latch MemoryPhi, whose incoming accesses are the
  unique writer MemoryDef and the same header MemoryPhi pass-through. The v3
  transcript records guard predicate/operands/polarity, both MemoryPhi nodes,
  and a matching guard variable/result on every F412-style byte-lane witness.

  Consumer validation rebuilds direct CFG predecessors/successors and block
  dominance, binds the comparison to its actual definition and available
  operands, validates writer/skip/latch identities and the unique store,
  independently reconstructs scalar operand widths and constant ranges, and replays the
  complete/reachable alias domains, no-wrap proof, object owner,
  and lane Cartesian product. Missing capabilities, a conditional capability
  without a v3 use point, raw-i1 or late guard operands, wrong polarity,
  two-writer arms, skip-path memory effects, multiple latches, nested
  predicates, pointer unions, and unknown memory effects fail closed.

  The certificate identifies potential guarded writer provenance only. The
  runtime byte-init bitmap remains authoritative and is updated only when the
  real writer edge executes; zero-trip, guard-false, early-exit, and
  insufficient-prefix loads remain infeasible. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Conditional_Loop_MemoryPhi_Guard_Carry_F413_2026-08-16.md``.

- F414 multi-latch MemoryPhi SCC finite fixed-point proof:

  This feature adds no ``SYMCC_*`` runtime switch. The producer activates it
  automatically only when a dynamic stack/ordinary-heap load is reached from
  a header MemoryPhi with two through four direct latch incoming edges. The
  artifact declares ``bounded-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction`` and
  ``bounded-multilatch-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-fixed-point`` and uses
  ``symcc-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction-v4``. General step, affine scale,
  or multi-byte writers additionally declare the F412 strided capability.

  The accepted loop has one preheader/header/exit and a join-free binary
  decision tree with at most three integer comparisons and two through four
  leaf latches. Every latch contributes the same ``add(iv, step)`` recurrence;
  each MemoryPhi backedge is either a direct, unique simple writer MemoryDef or
  the same header MemoryPhi carry. Decision operands are recursively limited
  to bounded arguments, non-negative constants, integer casts/binary
  operations/comparisons/selects, and defined non-poison freeze values.
  Loads, calls, pointer-derived scalars, nondeterministic freeze values,
  additional loop memory effects, mismatched steps, joins, side entries,
  nested loops, more than four latches, and unknown provenance fail closed.

  The producer computes a finite monotone union over recurrence-reachable
  writer byte lanes and records every round, a zero-growth stability round,
  and every writer alternative for every load alias/lane. Transfers are
  mutually exclusive backedge choices, never sequential effects. The Python
  consumer rebuilds CFG/dominance, scalar PHI edge copies, comparisons,
  MemoryPhi incoming identities, stores, aliases, object ownership, capacity,
  no-wrap facts, the fixed point, and the complete witness Cartesian product.
  Missing/dangling capabilities, bool-valued integer fields, reordered or
  partial alternatives, and synchronized transcript/program mutations fail
  before executor creation.

  The certificate describes potential writer provenance only. It never marks
  memory initialized: the runtime path-local byte-init bitmap is still the
  authority for zero-trip, carry, writer choice, early-exit, and insufficient
  prefix behavior. Bounds are 2,048 load lanes and 8,192 witness alternatives.
  Reference tools are:

      python3 benchmark/check_multilatch_loop_memoryphi_oracles.py
      python3 benchmark/benchmark_multilatch_loop_memoryphi.py
      python3 benchmark/generate_multilatch_loop_memoryphi_fixture.py \
        --object-bytes 64 --stride 8 --writer-bytes 8 --load-bytes 8

  See
  ``codex/research-progress/Multi_Latch_MemoryPhi_Fixed_Point_F414_2026-08-16.md``.

- F415 ordered multi-writer backedge transfer proof:

  This feature adds no ``SYMCC_*`` runtime switch. It extends F414 when one
  or more latch incoming definitions contain an ordered chain of two through
  four simple scalar stores. The whole loop may contain at most 16 stores.
  Such artifacts use ``symcc-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction-v5`` and add
  ``bounded-multilatch-loop-memoryphi-ordered-writer-transfer``; the base
  loop and multi-latch capabilities remain mandatory, and generalized affine
  writers still require the F412 strided capability. All-singleton shapes
  retain the v4 schema and byte-compatible transcript.

  The producer walks each header MemoryPhi latch incoming backward through
  its MemoryDef chain to the same header, reverses the chain, and verifies
  LLVM program order. Every writer independently binds its real store
  ordinal, width, affine pointer scale, complete alias domain, recurrence-
  reachable domain, and stack/heap owner. Any non-store definition, memory
  use, access outside the latch, unrecorded loop memory effect, fifth writer
  in one transfer, or seventeenth writer in the loop fails closed.

  Different backedge transfers remain mutually exclusive. Stores inside one
  transfer execute in order and v5 lane alternatives include both transfer
  and writer ordinals. The consumer binds this sequence to the actual lowered
  store list, requires ``ordered-writer-memory-def-chain`` incoming kinds,
  and independently rebuilds the fixed point and complete alternatives.
  The transcript summarizes initializedness provenance, not store values.
  The runtime path-local byte-init bitmap remains authoritative and is updated
  only by stores on the actually selected path.

  Reference tools are:

      python3 benchmark/check_ordered_multilatch_memoryphi_oracles.py
      python3 benchmark/benchmark_ordered_multilatch_memoryphi.py
      python3 benchmark/generate_ordered_multilatch_memoryphi_fixture.py \
        --object-bytes 64 --stride 8 --writer-bytes 8 --load-bytes 8 \
        --writers-per-transfer 4 --output /tmp/f415-boundary.ll

  See
  ``codex/research-progress/Ordered_MultiWriter_MemoryPhi_Transfer_F415_2026-08-16.md``.

- F416 nested-loop MemoryPhi summary composition:

  This feature adds no ``SYMCC_*`` runtime switch. It activates automatically
  for a finite dynamic load reached from an exact two-level reducible loop:
  the outer and inner loops each have one header and one latch, the inner exit
  enters the outer latch, and both unsigned induction variables start at zero
  with positive constant steps. Both bounds must be outer-loop invariant and
  derived from bounded integer inputs. General inner step, affine scale, or
  multi-byte writers additionally require the F412 strided capability.

  Accepted artifacts use
  ``symcc-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction-v6`` and declare
  ``bounded-nested-loop-memoryphi-summary-composition`` plus the base loop
  MemoryPhi capability. The outer MemoryPhi must merge live-on-entry with the
  inner MemoryPhi summary; the inner MemoryPhi must merge the outer version
  with an ordered chain of one through four simple stores from the inner body.
  Every writer binds its real store ordinal, width, positive affine pointer,
  complete alias domain, recurrence-reachable domain, and object owner. Any
  extra loop MemorySSA effect, third nesting level, outer-dependent inner
  bound, fifth writer, pointer union, or unsupported shape fails closed.

  The Python consumer independently rebuilds both CFG levels, four scalar PHI
  edge copies, both recurrences and guards, bound dominance, both MemoryPhi
  identities, the actual load/store pointer expressions, the finite lane
  closure, and every witness alternative. Static closure records potential
  initializedness provenance only. It never sets initialization state: the
  runtime path-local byte-init bitmap remains authoritative for zero outer
  trips, short inner prefixes, and actual writer execution. Bounds are 2,048
  load lanes and 8,192 witness alternatives.

  Reference tools are::

      python3 benchmark/check_nested_loop_memoryphi_summary_oracles.py
      python3 benchmark/benchmark_nested_loop_memoryphi_summary.py \
        --object-bytes 64 --outer-step 1 --inner-step 8 \
        --writer-widths 1,2,4,8 --load-bytes 8 \
        --repeats 11 --iterations 1000
      python3 benchmark/generate_nested_loop_memoryphi_summary_fixture.py \
        --object-bytes 64 --outer-step 1 --inner-step 8 \
        --writer-widths 1,2,4,8 --load-bytes 8 \
        --output /tmp/f416-boundary.ll

  See
  ``codex/research-progress/Nested_Loop_MemoryPhi_Summary_Composition_F416_2026-08-16.md``.

- F417 nested-loop MemoryPhi last-write value summary:

  This feature adds no ``SYMCC_*`` runtime switch. Within the complete F416
  support domain, it activates only when every ordered inner-body writer has a
  real byte-complete 8--64-bit integer constant operand. The producer upgrades the transcript to
  ``symcc-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction-v7`` and declares
  ``bounded-nested-loop-memoryphi-last-write-value-summary``. This capability
  requires the F416 nested-summary and base loop-MemoryPhi capabilities.

  Each writer record binds the actual store ordinal, integer bit width, signed
  constant operand, and one memory-order byte vector derived from LLVM
  ``DataLayout``. Each load lane contains a bounded ``last_write_cases`` list
  ordered by descending inner induction value and then descending writer
  ordinal. A case is enabled when the outer bound is positive and the inner
  bound is at least its ``minimum_inner_bound``. Selection is first-match;
  absence of a match means uninitialized, never an implicit zero value.

  The Python consumer reads the actual lowered store constant, independently
  reconstructs its bytes using the program endianness, rebuilds every case
  from the writer aliases and reachable domains, and recomputes ordering,
  threshold, and value byte. A dynamic writer value retains the valid v6
  initializedness summary but does not declare F417. The runtime still
  executes the real loop and uses the path-local byte-init bitmap as the final
  load-definedness authority; v7 is not currently a loop-skipping fast path.
  Non-byte-sized integers such as ``i1`` are outside the byte-exact proof
  boundary and fail closed instead of assigning semantics to padding bits.

  Reference tools are::

      python3 benchmark/check_nested_loop_memoryphi_value_summary_oracles.py
      python3 benchmark/benchmark_nested_loop_memoryphi_value_summary.py \
        --object-bytes 64 --inner-step 8 \
        --writer-widths 1,2,4,8 \
        --writer-values 17,4660,305419896,72623859790382856 \
        --load-bytes 8 --repeats 11 --iterations 1000
      python3 benchmark/generate_nested_loop_memoryphi_value_summary_fixture.py \
        --object-bytes 64 --inner-step 8 \
        --writer-widths 1,2,4,8 \
        --writer-values 17,4660,305419896,72623859790382856 \
        --load-bytes 8 --output /tmp/f417-boundary.ll

  See
  ``codex/research-progress/Nested_Loop_MemoryPhi_Last_Write_Value_Summary_F417_2026-08-16.md``.

- F418 nested-loop MemoryPhi two-dimensional affine summary:

  This feature adds no ``SYMCC_*`` runtime switch. It upgrades a complete
  two-level nested-loop proof when every writer address has the exact form
  ``base + pointer_scale * (constant + outer_scale * outer_iv +
  inner_scale * inner_iv)`` and every writer stores a byte-complete 8--64-bit
  integer constant. The producer emits
  ``symcc-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction-v8`` and declares
  ``bounded-nested-loop-memoryphi-two-dimensional-affine-summary`` together
  with its F416/F417 dependencies.

  Both loop bounds must originate in integer arguments narrower than 64 bits
  (an existing lowering ``zext`` is allowed). The producer enumerates the
  complete maximum domain, with at most 64 induction instances per dimension
  and at most 256 outer/inner pairs. Affine expressions admit only constants,
  the two induction variables, addition, and multiplication whose other
  operand is a direct positive integer literal; depth is capped at four.
  Every enumerated index/address must match the actual bounded alias map,
  remain in object bounds, and satisfy the existing witness/alternative
  budgets. ``SYMCC_LIVE_ALIAS_LIMIT`` therefore remains the external resource
  control; the dimensional limits are compile-time proof bounds rather than
  user-tunable execution policy.

  Each load-lane case is ordered by descending outer induction, inner
  induction, and writer ordinal. It carries both
  ``minimum_outer_bound = outer + 1`` and
  ``minimum_inner_bound = inner + 1`` plus the target-endian constant byte.
  The strict consumer reconstructs the actual affine expression, pointer,
  store, maximum-domain instances, pair closure, ordering, thresholds, and
  value bytes before executor creation. Dynamic values, non-affine products,
  mixed direct/2D writer shapes, overflow, incomplete aliases, and excessive
  domains fail closed. Runtime still executes both loops and retains the
  path-local byte-initialization bitmap as final load-definedness authority;
  v8 is not a loop-skipping optimization.

  Reference tools are::

      python3 benchmark/check_nested_loop_memoryphi_two_dimensional_affine_oracles.py
      python3 benchmark/benchmark_nested_loop_memoryphi_two_dimensional_affine.py \
        --object-bytes 24 --outer-step 1 --inner-step 2 \
        --outer-scale 8 --inner-scale 1 \
        --writer-widths 2,1 --writer-values 4660,170 \
        --load-bytes 2 --repeats 9 --iterations 500
      python3 benchmark/generate_nested_loop_memoryphi_two_dimensional_affine_fixture.py \
        --object-bytes 24 --outer-step 1 --inner-step 2 \
        --outer-scale 8 --inner-scale 1 \
        --writer-widths 2,1 --writer-values 4660,170 \
        --load-bytes 2 --output /tmp/f418-boundary.ll

  See
  ``codex/research-progress/Nested_Loop_MemoryPhi_Two_Dimensional_Affine_Summary_F418_2026-08-16.md``.

- F419 nested-loop MemoryPhi affine symbolic value summary:

  This feature adds no runtime environment variable. When an F418 exact
  two-dimensional address certificate has byte-complete integer stores, the
  producer may upgrade it to
  ``symcc-loop-memoryphi-byte-lane-induction-v9`` and emit
  ``bounded-nested-loop-memoryphi-affine-symbolic-value-summary``.

  A symbolic writer value must have the same integer width as the store and
  normalize to
  ``constant + outer_scale*outer + inner_scale*inner + input_scale*input``
  modulo ``2^bits``. Leaves are non-negative ConstantInt values, the two
  induction variables, and at most one entry integer argument. Internal nodes
  are addition or multiplication by a direct positive constant; depth is at
  most four and definitions must be in the inner body before the store.
  ``nuw/nsw``, subtraction, multiple inputs, cross-block values, excessive
  coefficients, and unsupported widths fail closed.

  For an input-dependent value the artifact records the exact argument SSA,
  input byte offset, and byte count. The strict consumer independently
  reconstructs the entry-block ``input/zext/shl/or/identity`` assembly and
  requires contiguous unique little-endian bytes. It also reconstructs the
  store SSA formula, F418 writer instances, double-bound last-write order, and
  target-endian byte extraction before executor creation.

  This certificate does not skip the loop, prefill memory, or replace stores.
  Real execution and the path-local initializedness bitmap remain authoritative.
  Reference tools are::

      python3 benchmark/check_nested_loop_memoryphi_affine_symbolic_value_oracles.py
      python3 benchmark/benchmark_nested_loop_memoryphi_affine_symbolic_value.py \
        --repeats 9 --iterations 500
      python3 benchmark/generate_nested_loop_memoryphi_affine_symbolic_value_fixture.py \
        --output /tmp/f419-boundary.ll

  The default generated fixture requires ``SYMCC_LIVE_ALIAS_LIMIT=64`` because
  it deliberately exercises a 24-byte bounded alias domain. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Nested_Loop_MemoryPhi_Affine_Symbolic_Value_Summary_F419_2026-08-17.md``.

- F306 interleaved fail-closed QA3 coverage attribution:

  This feature adds no `SYMCC_*` runtime switch. Coverage-reproduction tools
  use explicit command-line controls instead:

      python3 benchmark/run_qa3_coverage_campaign.py \
        --afl-binary <afl-target> --corpus <outputs> --seed <baseline> \
        --output <evidence-dir> --rounds 3 \
        --stability-policy strict --replica-delay-ms 1000

  Each campaign first compares persistent `afl-showmap -S` with an isolated
  one-shot observation using both the process status and the complete edge-ID
  set. A mismatch retires streaming mode and moves the whole campaign to
  one-shot measurements; the two oracle semantics are never mixed in one
  result. The streaming protocol caps input at 16 MiB, sparse edges at
  1,048,576, stdout and stderr at 4 MiB each, and gives every response a target
  timeout plus five seconds of read grace. Each query can restart the protocol
  at most once, including when it starts with a dead session. One-shot sparse
  maps reject duplicate edges and counts outside the u8 range; solver telemetry
  is read with a 1 MiB limit before its unsigned counters are validated.

  The default coverage policy is `strict`: three cyclically interleaved rounds
  must produce an identical edge set for every input. `intersection` is a
  conservative explicit alternative and `union` records possibly observed
  coverage. The auditable driver writes every replica edge set, round ordering,
  tool/input hashes, a derived summary, and `SHA256SUMS.txt`. These controls
  affect benchmark attribution only; they do not change worker symbolic
  execution or AFL corpus-admission semantics. See
  `codex/Interleaved_Fail_Closed_Coverage_Oracle_2026-08-05.md`.

- F374 Agolic-style run-level bounded-execution planning:

  This feature is a Python orchestration API, not a process-global
  `SYMCC_*` switch. Import `AgolicRunLevelPlanner` and
  `AgolicRoundController` from `util/agolic_planning.py`. A profile supplies
  the admitted environment, symbolic input surface, supported execution
  modes, time limit, and memory limit. The controller requires separate
  callbacks for round-start corpus replay, bounded worker execution, and
  per-result native replay. Worker execution may run in parallel, but
  `replay_run(plan, run_result, prior_coverage)` is deliberately serialized
  in completion order and must return cumulative replay coverage together
  with the current run's `generated_artifacts`.

  The state file has a single-writer coordinator contract and is bound to the
  experiment ID, program ID, and executable SHA-256. An exact completed run
  specification is never reissued. History is not truncated: exhausting the
  configured capacity fails closed and requires campaign archival. Coverage
  advances only after concrete replay; a reused replay identity, a
  non-monotonic cumulative snapshot, an unreviewed witness, an unknown
  proposal field, or a worker reach claim that disagrees with replay is
  rejected without consuming the pending plan. See
  `codex/research-progress/Agolic_Run_Level_Planning_F374_2026-08-12.md`.

- F423 executable Agolic witness-guided BSE runner:

  This feature is an explicit Python runner over a lowered live-continuation
  program; it has no implicit process-global enable switch. A plan must first
  be admitted by the F374 planner and must bind the exact program SHA-256,
  target function/branch, reviewed witness, release boundary, resource profile,
  and `seed_hex` symbolic-input surface. Run it in an isolated process as::

      python3 util/agolic_bse_runner.py \
        --program /path/to/program.json \
        --program-sha256 <sha256> \
        --workspace /path/to/agolic-workspace \
        --max-steps 100000 --max-states 4096 --max-artifacts 256 \
        execute /path/to/run-plan.json > /path/to/bse-result.json

      python3 util/agolic_bse_runner.py \
        --program /path/to/program.json \
        --program-sha256 <sha256> \
        --workspace /path/to/agolic-workspace \
        replay /path/to/run-plan.json /path/to/bse-result.json \
        > /path/to/replay-result.json

      python3 util/agolic_bse_runner.py \
        --program /path/to/program.json \
        --program-sha256 <sha256> \
        --workspace /path/to/agolic-workspace coverage

  The plan's bounded time and memory limits remain authoritative. Non-empty
  admitted `SYMCC_*` profile environment is accepted only by the isolated CLI
  worker; the in-process API rejects it to prevent parallel plans from racing
  on process-global environment. The runner persists pre-release/released,
  mode, and release-configuration markers in checkpoints. Before release,
  witness-guided execution is one-state with zero feasibility queries; after
  release it restores ordinary solver-backed exploration.

  Worker candidates are content-addressed under
  `workspace/runs/<plan-id>/candidates/` and are not corpus or coverage facts.
  `replay` revalidates all identities, performs concrete-only entry-to-terminal
  execution with zero forks and solver queries, then publishes accepted bytes
  under `workspace/corpus/`. The cumulative coverage snapshot is rebuilt from
  entered functions and actual `branch:<site>:T/F` outcomes. This is a bounded
  continuation capability, not a native KLEE-equivalence or public-campaign
  performance claim. See
  `codex/research-progress/Executable_Agolic_Witness_Guided_BSE_Runner_F423_2026-08-17.md`.

- F427 independently checked QF_BV UNSAT proof receipts:

  Configure proof generation per ``smtlib-qfbv`` portfolio entry with the
  ``unsat_proof`` object. The required fields are ``generator_command`` with a
  ``{query}`` placeholder, ``checker_command`` with a ``{proof}`` placeholder,
  and ``signature_root`` containing ``Cpc.eo`` plus
  ``expert/CpcExpert.eo``. Only ``format: cpc`` is accepted. The optional
  ``generator_trusted_files`` and ``checker_trusted_files`` lists bind wrapper
  scripts or other local artifacts by content. ``timeout_ms`` defaults to
  30000 and is bounded to 1--3600000 milliseconds.

  The tested cvc5 generator arguments are::

      --lang=smt2 --safe-mode=safe \
      --proof-granularity=dsl-rewrite --dump-proofs {query}

  Install the pinned cvc5 1.3.4/Ethos/CPC bundle with::

      benchmark/install_cvc5_cpc_ethos_1_3_4.sh

  Store controls are available as service arguments or environment variables::

      --qfbv-proof-store PATH
      --qfbv-proof-max-objects N
      --qfbv-proof-max-bytes N

      SYMCC_QFBV_PROOF_STORE=PATH
      SYMCC_QFBV_PROOF_MAX_OBJECTS=1000000
      SYMCC_QFBV_PROOF_MAX_BYTES=67108864

  ``max_objects`` is in 1--10000000 and counts indexed proof plus receipt
  objects. ``max_bytes`` is in 1024--67108864 and bounds each portable proof
  body. If a portfolio enables proof receipts but no path is supplied, the
  service uses ``<query-store>/qfbv-unsat-proofs``. Configure an explicitly
  qualified shared filesystem path for cross-node reuse.

  A configured proof verifier overrides the legacy ``accept_unsat`` shortcut:
  raw backend UNSAT becomes authoritative only after cvc5 CPC generation,
  exact Ethos ``correct`` output, CAS publication, and independent QueryStore
  replay. Receipt reuse skips the primary solver and generator but always
  re-runs the local checker. Generator, checker, all CPC ``.eo`` signatures,
  Query IR lowering, capabilities, F426 context, reference problem, proof, and
  result are content-bound. Any drift, ``incomplete`` proof, timeout,
  cancellation, quota failure, or artifact mismatch returns ``unknown``.

  With no ``unsat_proof`` object the feature is disabled: the existing
  one-shot and persistent QF_BV paths perform one normal lowering and do not
  scan proof tools or stores. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Proof_Carrying_QFBV_UNSAT_Receipts_F427_2026-08-17.md``.

- F428 verified QF_BV learned lemma exchange:

  Add ``learned_lemmas`` to a persistent ``smtlib-qfbv`` portfolio entry that
  already has ``unsat_proof`` configured::

      "learned_lemmas": {
        "type": "preprocess",
        "max_per_query": 8,
        "timeout_ms": 30000
      }

  ``type`` is one of ``preprocess``, ``input``, ``solvable``, or ``internal``.
  ``max_per_query`` is in 1--64 and ``timeout_ms`` is in 1--3600000. The
  feature requires a persistent solver with ``incremental: true``, an F426
  context store, and an F427 CPC/Ethos proof verifier. Configuration fails
  closed if any prerequisite is absent.

  Configure the record store with service arguments or environment variables::

      --qfbv-lemma-store PATH
      --qfbv-lemma-max-records N
      --qfbv-lemma-max-bytes N

      SYMCC_QFBV_LEMMA_STORE=PATH
      SYMCC_QFBV_LEMMA_MAX_RECORDS=1000000
      SYMCC_QFBV_LEMMA_MAX_BYTES=268435456

  ``max_records`` is in 1--10000000. ``max_bytes`` is in 1048576--
  1099511627776 and bounds total indexed record bytes; each record remains
  bounded to 1 MiB. If learned lemmas are enabled without an explicit path,
  the service uses ``<query-store>/qfbv-verified-lemmas``. ``--stats`` and
  ``--once`` summaries expose ``qfbv_lemma_store`` cardinality and bytes.

  cvc5 candidate output is not trusted. The backend proves
  ``source-prefix AND NOT lemma`` UNSAT with the configured CPC/Ethos chain,
  and only exact descendant prefixes may consume the record. The consumer and
  QueryStore independently reconstruct and check the receipt before injection
  or publication accounting. Failure to extract or publish a lemma does not
  invalidate an otherwise replay-verified SAT result, but no unverified lemma
  is asserted. This mode performs an additional one-shot full-formula cvc5
  pass after SAT; account for it in experiments. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Verified_QFBV_Lemma_Exchange_F428_2026-08-17.md``.

- F429 cross-job QF_BV artifact lifecycle and bounded GC:

  Lifecycle deletion is opt-in. Configure the shared registry explicitly with::

      --qfbv-artifact-lifecycle-store PATH
      --qfbv-artifact-job-id ID
      --qfbv-artifact-job-lease-seconds 60
      --qfbv-artifact-max-jobs 1000000
      --qfbv-artifact-gc-on-start
      --qfbv-artifact-gc-grace-seconds 86400
      --qfbv-artifact-gc-max-objects 1024
      --qfbv-artifact-gc-max-bytes 268435456
      --qfbv-artifact-gc-time-ms 30000
      --qfbv-artifact-gc-scan-max-objects 100000

  The corresponding environment variables are
  ``SYMCC_QFBV_ARTIFACT_LIFECYCLE_STORE``,
  ``SYMCC_QFBV_ARTIFACT_JOB_ID``,
  ``SYMCC_QFBV_ARTIFACT_JOB_LEASE_SECONDS``,
  ``SYMCC_QFBV_ARTIFACT_MAX_JOBS``,
  ``SYMCC_QFBV_ARTIFACT_GC_GRACE_SECONDS``,
  ``SYMCC_QFBV_ARTIFACT_GC_MAX_OBJECTS``,
  ``SYMCC_QFBV_ARTIFACT_GC_MAX_BYTES``,
  ``SYMCC_QFBV_ARTIFACT_GC_TIME_MS``, and
  ``SYMCC_QFBV_ARTIFACT_GC_SCAN_MAX_OBJECTS``.

  ``max_jobs`` is in 1--10000000 and is a persistent store identity. Job rows
  retain their last generation so a stale lease cannot become valid after
  metadata reclamation; once distinct IDs reach the bound, new IDs fail while
  existing IDs may start a higher generation. The lease is in 0.1--86400
  seconds. Grace is in 0--31536000 seconds. One collection admits at most
  1--1000000 objects, 1--1099511627776 encoded bytes, and a 1--3600000 ms
  lock/selection budget. An admitted single-object recoverable deletion may
  finish after that selection deadline.

  ``--qfbv-artifact-gc-only`` synchronizes every configured context,
  proof/receipt, and lemma index, performs one collection, prints JSON, and
  exits. If the combined indexed count exceeds
  ``--qfbv-artifact-gc-scan-max-objects`` (1--3000000), synchronization is
  incomplete and the service deletes nothing. Managed stores reject access
  without the same lifecycle registry, preventing an uncoordinated process
  from racing the collector.

  The dependency graph is ``context -> parent``, ``receipt -> proof``, and
  ``lemma -> source-context + receipt``. Active fenced job references and
  recent ``last_seen`` entries are roots; recursive mark protects their
  dependencies. Sweep deletes unreachable dependents before dependencies.
  Missing graph endpoints, dependency cycles, stale generations, store
  identity drift, and non-finite time values fail closed. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Lease_Fenced_QFBV_Artifact_Lifecycle_F429_2026-08-17.md``.

- F430 same-host native Z3 state reuse for persistent QF_BV backends:

  Build the Linux-only helper against an explicitly selected Z3 distribution
  when reproducible solver identity matters::

      cmake -S . -B build \
        -DSYMCC_BUILD_QFBV_Z3_FORKSERVER=ON \
        -DSYMCC_QFBV_Z3_ROOT=/opt/z3-5.0.0
      cmake --build build --target symcc_qfbv_z3_forkserver

  If ``SYMCC_QFBV_Z3_ROOT`` is empty, CMake uses system Z3 development files.
  The target is omitted on non-Linux systems or when those files are absent.
  Enable the mechanism explicitly on one persistent ``smtlib-qfbv`` portfolio
  entry::

      {
        "name": "z3-native-qfbv",
        "kind": "smtlib-qfbv",
        "command": ["/path/to/symcc-qfbv-z3-forkserver"],
        "persistent": true,
        "native_state_fork": true,
        "prefix_cache": 4,
        "capabilities": {
          "incremental": true,
          "accept_unsat": false
        }
      }

  ``native_state_fork`` must be a JSON Boolean. It is rejected for one-shot or
  non-QF_BV entries and cannot share an entry with verified cvc5 learned-literal
  publication. Put those mechanisms in separate portfolio entries if both are
  required. The helper warms a single-thread Z3 parent at each dirty prefix and
  solves every target in a Linux copy-on-write child. A target timeout kills and
  reaps only that child; malformed or regressing helper metadata evicts the
  entire cached parent. Portfolio cancellation still terminates the process
  group and therefore discards the parent snapshot.

  This is an ephemeral same-host optimization, not a serialized solver-state
  ABI. It does not enter the F429 artifact store, cross a node or service
  restart, or change the F426 capability/context digest. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Native_QFBV_Solver_State_Fork_Reuse_F430_2026-08-17.md``.

- F431 verified variable-substitution QF_BV UNSAT-core reuse:

  Add ``substitution_cores`` to an ``smtlib-qfbv`` portfolio entry that already
  configures the F427 ``unsat_proof`` verifier::

      "substitution_cores": {
        "extractor_command": [
          "cvc5", "--lang=smt2", "--safe-mode=safe", "{query}"
        ],
        "max_candidates": 64,
        "max_candidate_scan": 4096,
        "max_join_states": 65536,
        "max_unification_pairs": 1000000,
        "verified_core_cache_entries": 1024,
        "lookup_timeout_ms": 5000,
        "publish_timeout_ms": 30000
      }

  ``extractor_command`` is required and must contain one ``{query}``
  placeholder. Candidate count is in 1--4096; scan count is between candidate
  count and 1000000; join states and unification pairs are in 1--100000000;
  verified-cache entries are in 1--65536; both timeouts are in 1--3600000 ms.
  The feature fails configuration when no proof verifier is present.

  Configure the content-addressed record store with arguments or environment::

      --qfbv-substitution-core-store PATH
      --qfbv-substitution-core-max-records 100000
      --qfbv-substitution-core-max-bytes 1073741824

      SYMCC_QFBV_SUBSTITUTION_CORE_STORE=PATH
      SYMCC_QFBV_SUBSTITUTION_CORE_MAX_RECORDS=100000
      SYMCC_QFBV_SUBSTITUTION_CORE_MAX_BYTES=1073741824

  Records are limited to 128 MiB each. ``max_records`` is in 1--10000000 and
  total bytes are in 134217728--1099511627776. With no explicit path, enabled
  entries use ``<query-store>/qfbv-substitution-cores``. Managed lifecycle mode
  records ``core -> receipt -> proof`` dependencies and includes cores in
  bounded GC synchronization.

  Bloom filters and structural footprints only reject impossible candidates.
  A hit requires exact source-byte to target-byte unification, recomputation of
  every substituted core root, target-root inclusion, source CPC/Ethos replay,
  and an independent QueryStore replay. The first proof check is cached only
  inside one process and verification domain; a fresh process checks it again.
  Any timeout, cancellation, malformed extractor response, mapping conflict,
  proof drift, quota error, or CAS mismatch falls back to the normal solver.

  The current substitution domain is 8-bit Query IR input reads, not arbitrary
  SMT sorts. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Variable_Substitution_UNSAT_Core_Reuse_F431_2026-08-17.md``.

- F432 verified incremental QF_BV SAT and persistent proof fragments:

  Install the pinned CaDiCaL 3.0.1 CLI and shared library with::

      bash benchmark/install_cadical_3_0_1.sh

  Add a dedicated portfolio entry. Persistent mode requires an explicit native
  library and an incremental capability contract::

      {
        "name": "cadical-3.0.1-native",
        "kind": "bitblast-cadical-qfbv",
        "persistent": true,
        "native_library": "/path/to/libcadical.so",
        "command": [
          "/path/to/cadical", "--plain", "--lrat", "--no-binary",
          "{cnf}", "{proof}"
        ],
        "capabilities": {"incremental": true},
        "max_imported_clauses": 64,
        "context_cache": 8,
        "worker_epoch": 0
      }

  ``max_imported_clauses`` is in 0--4096, ``context_cache`` is in 1--64,
  and ``worker_epoch`` is a nonnegative signed 64-bit value. One-shot mode sets
  ``persistent`` false and omits ``native_library``. Both modes require the
  command placeholders and the ``--plain --lrat --no-binary`` flags.

  Configure the proof-fragment CAS with arguments or environment::

      --qfbv-incremental-proof-store PATH
      --qfbv-incremental-proof-max-records 1000000
      --qfbv-incremental-proof-max-bytes 68719476736

      SYMCC_QFBV_INCREMENTAL_PROOF_STORE=PATH
      SYMCC_QFBV_INCREMENTAL_PROOF_MAX_RECORDS=1000000
      SYMCC_QFBV_INCREMENTAL_PROOF_MAX_BYTES=68719476736

  With no explicit path, an enabled entry uses
  ``<query-store>/qfbv-incremental-proofs``. Records are immutable and all
  imports are recursively LRUP-checked before injection. Managed lifecycle mode
  tracks them as ``sat-proof`` objects and preserves import dependencies during
  bounded GC.

  Do not mix ``unsat_proof``, ``learned_lemmas``, ``substitution_cores``, or
  ``native_state_fork`` into the same CaDiCaL entry. Those options belong to the
  SMT backend and use different state and proof identities. The base F432 path
  exchanges clauses synchronously at solve boundaries. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Verified_Incremental_QFBV_SAT_and_Proof_DAG_F432_2026-08-17.md``.

- F433 opt-in realtime checked QF_BV proof stream:

  The pinned installer now also builds
  ``libsymcc_qfbv_cadical_realtime.so``. Add ``realtime_stream`` only to a
  persistent CaDiCaL entry::

      "realtime_stream": {
        "library": "/path/to/libsymcc_qfbv_cadical_realtime.so",
        "max_imports": 64,
        "max_events": 4096,
        "max_learned": 64,
        "max_learned_length": 32,
        "poll_interval_ms": 2,
        "checker_budget_ms": 100,
        "native_queue_literals": 65536
      }

  The object has a strict field whitelist. Bounds are: ``max_imports``
  0--4096, ``max_events`` 1--1000000, ``max_learned`` and
  ``max_learned_length`` 0--65536, ``poll_interval_ms`` 1--1000,
  ``checker_budget_ms`` 1--60000, and ``native_queue_literals``
  1--16777216. Boolean values are not accepted as integers. Zero imports or
  learned clauses disables that native queue exactly.

  The realtime shim and ``native_library`` must embed the same CaDiCaL 3.0.x
  signature. During solve, only independently LRUP/RUP-checked clauses enter
  the native queue; a clause is reported as delivered only after CaDiCaL's
  external-clause callback consumes its terminator. QueryStore independently
  replays the ACK, durable event, record, and proof before accepting a result.

  Omit ``realtime_stream`` for the unchanged F432 C/IPASIR behavior. Realtime
  mode has a measurable 2--3 ms fixed session cost on very short warm queries;
  enable it for parallel long-solve experiments, not as an unconditional
  single-query latency optimization. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Realtime_Checked_QFBV_Proof_Stream_F433_2026-08-18.md``.

- F434 qualified multi-rank realtime proof evaluation:

  F434 is an experiment driver, not a new default runtime switch. Launch it
  under MPI with the exact realtime shim used by the worker portfolio::

      mpiexec -n 5 python3 benchmark/run_qfbv_realtime_multirank.py \
        --library /path/to/libsymcc_qfbv_cadical_realtime.so \
        --proof-root /shared/f434-runs \
        --publishers 2 --rounds 2 \
        --variables 200 --clauses 860 \
        --mode active --output /shared/f434-active.json

  Rank 0 is coordinator-only; ranks ``1..publishers`` are publishers and all
  remaining ranks are consumers. At least one publisher and one consumer are
  mandatory. ``active`` requires every consumer to expose native
  ``solve_generation=1`` and ``solving=1`` before publishers are released.
  ``preloaded`` exercises the same CAS/checker/ACK path but starts solve after
  imports have entered the queue, so it is only a protocol baseline.

  The proof root is always extended with a fresh random run directory. All
  ranks probe its durable filesystem operations. Physically colocated ranks
  are identified with the MPI shared-memory communicator; multi-host jobs must
  pass cross-host advisory-lock and namespace qualification. A path that is
  merely spelled the same on every host is not accepted as shared state.

  Bounds are: world size 3--16384, publishers 1--``world_size-2``, rounds
  1--4096, SAT variables 8--4096, clauses from ``variables`` through 50000,
  solve timeout 100--3600000 ms, and poll interval 1--1000 ms. The default
  200-variable/860-clause random 3-SAT workload exists to keep a real CDCL
  solve active; it is not an application benchmark. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Qualified_Multirank_Realtime_Proof_Evaluation_F434_2026-08-18.md``.

- F435 opt-in adaptive checked-proof admission:

  Add an ``adaptive`` object inside the F433 ``realtime_stream`` object. It is
  deliberately not enabled by merely selecting realtime mode::

      "realtime_stream": {
        "library": "/path/to/libsymcc_qfbv_cadical_realtime.so",
        "max_imports": 64,
        "adaptive": {
          "high_watermark_permille": 750,
          "min_score": 1500,
          "max_deferred": 128,
          "max_retries": 4,
          "max_decisions": 4096,
          "max_clause_literals": 32,
          "checker_reference_us": 1000,
          "age_ramp_ms": 100,
          "minimum_remaining_ms": 2
        }
      }

  The native queue capacity is inherited from ``max_imports`` and cannot be
  restated inside ``adaptive``. Bounds are: high watermark 1--1000 permille,
  score -10000--10000, deferred capacity 1--4096, retries 1--32, decisions
  1--1000000, clause literals 1--65536, checker reference 1--60000000 us,
  age ramp 1--3600000 ms, and minimum remaining time 0--3600000 ms. Unknown
  keys, booleans used as integers, and out-of-range values fail configuration.

  Every record is still independently LRUP/RUP checked before admission. The
  deterministic controller can admit, defer with bounded exponential poll
  backoff, or reject by a hard resource limit; it cannot authorize an unchecked
  clause or a SAT/UNSAT result. Native ACK, backpressure, and solve-end expiry
  update persistent per-source feedback. QueryStore replays the sealed decision
  and independently reloads the proof and durable event. CaDiCaL's current
  Learner callback does not expose an auditable LBD in this bridge, so F435 does
  not synthesize or claim LBD-aware sharing. See
  ``codex/research-progress/Adaptive_Checked_Proof_Admission_F435_2026-08-18.md``.

- F436 opt-in native clause-activity telemetry:

  Add the exact JSON boolean ``track_clause_activity`` to the F433
  ``realtime_stream`` object. It is false by default and may be combined with
  F435 adaptive admission::

      "realtime_stream": {
        "library": "/path/to/libsymcc_qfbv_cadical_realtime.so",
        "max_imports": 64,
        "track_clause_activity": true,
        "adaptive": {
          "high_watermark_permille": 750,
          "min_score": 1500
        }
      }

  When enabled, the shim must negotiate
  ``symcc-qfbv-native-clause-activity-v1`` and expose the protocol, enable and
  dequeue symbols as one complete capability. A partial ABI, a non-boolean
  value, malformed receipt, non-contiguous activity ordinal, unresolved ACK,
  non-quiescent solve, or accounting mismatch fails closed.

  Only proof-checked clauses that receive a native delivery ACK are tracked.
  The first observed state with no satisfied literals and one unassigned
  literal emits a ``unit`` receipt; zero unassigned literals emits
  ``conflict``. A delivered clause that never reaches either state is counted
  as ``unactivated``. Receipts bind the proof record, durable event, ACK token,
  generation, clause identity, decision level, unit literal and exact
  falsifying witness. QueryStore independently reconstructs the Query IR,
  proof, event, ACK and witness before accepting the telemetry.

  This is bounded semantic-activation telemetry, not proof that an imported
  clause uniquely caused a propagation or reduced solve time. It is intended
  for long-solve parallel experiments and future uncertainty-aware pairing,
  not unconditional single-query optimization.

  To collect qualified local MPI evidence::

      mpiexec -n 5 python3 benchmark/run_qfbv_realtime_multirank.py \
        --library /path/to/libsymcc_qfbv_cadical_realtime.so \
        --proof-root /shared/f436-runs \
        --publishers 2 --rounds 1 --variables 200 --clauses 860 \
        --mode active --track-clause-activity \
        --output /shared/f436-active.json

  See
  ``codex/research-progress/Native_Clause_Activity_and_Utility_Feedback_F436_2026-08-18.md``.

F437 utility-aware checked-proof worker pairing
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

The persistent CaDiCaL QF_BV portfolio accepts an optional ``pairing`` object
inside ``realtime_stream``. Pairing requires both realtime mode and
``track_clause_activity: true`` because native unit/conflict activity is the
only positive semantic feedback used by this controller. Example::

  {
    "realtime_stream": {
      "library": "/path/to/libsymcc_qfbv_cadical_realtime.so",
      "track_clause_activity": true,
      "pairing": {
        "min_exploration_samples": 2,
        "refresh_after_events": 128,
        "min_score": 3250,
        "uncertainty_scale": 2000,
        "checker_reference_us": 1000,
        "event_lag_reference": 64,
        "unit_reward": 4500,
        "conflict_reward": 5000,
        "unactivated_reward": 500,
        "backpressure_reward": -500,
        "expired_reward": -250,
        "max_pairs": 4096,
        "max_events": 1000000
      }
    }
  }

Unknown fields, booleans in integer fields, unsupported bounds, pairing
without activity tracking, and activity tracking without a capable native ABI
fail during configuration. The key is
``publisher_worker x consumer_worker x formula_family_sha256``. The family is
a value-insensitive protocol/size/operator-count signature, not an expression
graph equivalence proof. QueryStore persists only independently replayed,
quiescent snapshots and the backend restores the snapshot for the same worker
identity and policy SHA-256.

This option changes checked-clause delivery policy. It does not waive LRUP/RUP
checking, authorize an UNSAT result, or make an unactivated clause a proven
negative contribution. See
``codex/research-progress/Utility_Aware_Proof_Worker_Pairing_F437_2026-08-18.md``.

F438 generation-fenced malleable query workers
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

The query service can logically resize a fixed, prelaunched ``--jobs`` pool::

  python3 util/symcc_query_service.py \
    --store /shared/query-store --jobs 8 \
    --malleable-workers \
    --malleable-pool-id node-a-qfbv \
    --malleable-backlog-per-slot 2 \
    --malleable-hysteresis-slots 1 \
    --portfolio portfolio.json

``--malleable-workers`` is opt-in. ``--jobs`` remains the physical thread
capacity; the controller changes only the active logical assignments.
``--malleable-backlog-per-slot`` must be in 1--1000000. Hysteresis must be in
0--``--jobs``. A non-empty pool ID must remain stable for the same QueryStore,
policy and worker inventory. The default pool ID is derived from the store
path.

Reassignment is generation fenced. A worker first stops accepting new claims,
retires the exact QueryStore lease-token set, seals any proof artifacts
finished during the drain, and emits a drain receipt. Only then can commit
issue a new assignment token. Process-local solver liveness takes precedence
over QueryStore timeout during an online drain; after restart the durable
QueryStore token is authoritative. A lifetime advisory lock prevents two
service processes from coordinating the same pool ID.

This mode implements logical grow/drain/migrate/shrink over prelaunched slots.
It does not dynamically create MPI ranks or repair a failed communicator. See
``codex/research-progress/Generation_Fenced_Malleable_Worker_Pool_F438_2026-08-18.md``.

F439 strict LIDRUP proof-wire interoperability
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

The F432 CaDiCaL QF_BV proof path can additionally export every authorized
UNSAT result as matched ICNF/LIDRUP files and verify them with the pinned
``lidrup-check`` 0.0.7 executable. Install the fixed source revision with::

  bash benchmark/install_lidrup_check_0_0_7.sh

Enable the external trust boundary by configuring the executable and its
exact SHA-256 identity together::

  python3 util/symcc_query_service.py \
    --store /shared/query-store --portfolio portfolio.json \
    --qfbv-incremental-proof-store /shared/qfbv-proofs \
    --qfbv-lidrup-checker /opt/lidrup-check \
    --qfbv-lidrup-checker-sha256 HEX \
    --qfbv-lidrup-wire-store /shared/qfbv-lidrup-wire \
    --qfbv-lidrup-wire-max-records 100000 \
    --qfbv-lidrup-wire-max-bytes 4294967296 \
    --qfbv-lidrup-timeout-ms 30000

The equivalent environment variables are
``SYMCC_QFBV_LIDRUP_CHECKER``,
``SYMCC_QFBV_LIDRUP_CHECKER_SHA256``,
``SYMCC_QFBV_LIDRUP_WIRE_STORE``,
``SYMCC_QFBV_LIDRUP_WIRE_MAX_RECORDS``,
``SYMCC_QFBV_LIDRUP_WIRE_MAX_BYTES`` and
``SYMCC_QFBV_LIDRUP_TIMEOUT_MS``. Checker path and digest are inseparable;
the wire store cannot be enabled without them, and the incremental proof store
is mandatory. If the wire-store path is omitted, the service uses
``<query-store>/qfbv-lidrup-wire``.

The backend first verifies the project LRUP DAG, deterministically flattens
recursive imports, emits a matched interaction/proof pair, and invokes a
private executable snapshot with ``--strict``. QueryStore reloads the immutable
sidecar and runs the external checker again before commit. A checker timeout,
output overflow, unexpected stderr/status, identity change, malformed wire,
CAS mismatch or quota failure turns an UNSAT answer into ``unknown`` or rejects
the result; it never falls back to trusting the producer.

The historical ``symcc-qfbv-palrup-proof-fragment-v1`` value denotes the
project JSON LRUP DAG and is retained only for record-hash compatibility.
F439 separately implements the official PalRUP ``a/i/d`` binary fragment
codec and cross-checks it with the pinned official converter installed by::

  bash benchmark/install_palrup_check_sat2026.sh

That converter oracle proves fragment syntax interoperability only. It does
not authorize global UNSAT and does not implement PalRUP's multi-worker
local-check/redistribute/confirm procedure. See
``codex/research-progress/LIDRUP_PalRUP_Proof_Wire_Interoperability_F439_2026-08-18.md``.

F440 PalRUP global confirmation pipeline
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

F440 provides an explicit library and CLI for the complete official PalRUP
checker sequence. Install the pinned SAT 2026 artifact first::

  bash benchmark/install_palrup_check_sat2026.sh

Then provide the formula, native proof-fragment root, solver count and exact
content identities for all three official executables::

  python3 util/qfbv_palrup_pipeline.py \
    --formula /data/input.cnf \
    --proof-root /data/input.palrup \
    --num-solvers 12 \
    --local-check "$HOME/.local/opt/palrup-check-sat2026/bin/palrup_local_check" \
    --redistribute "$HOME/.local/opt/palrup-check-sat2026/bin/palrup_redistribute" \
    --confirm "$HOME/.local/opt/palrup-check-sat2026/bin/palrup_confirm" \
    --local-check-sha256 HEX \
    --redistribute-sha256 HEX \
    --confirm-sha256 HEX \
    --timeout-ms 300000 --max-parallel 4 \
    --output /data/input.palrup-global-receipt.json

The checker snapshots the nonempty formula and all ``N`` fragments, runs
``N`` local checks, ``ceil(sqrt(N))^2`` redistribution cells, then ``N``
confirmations. The square count follows the pinned implementation's
``test_full_run.c`` and ``pal.sh`` execution contract. Success requires the
exact ``N`` confirmation directories, a nonempty canonical UNSAT-witness set,
all expected hash/proxy/import files, and task/rank/byte conservation.

``--timeout-ms`` is the deadline of each checker process, not a whole-pipeline
deadline. ``--max-parallel`` bounds concurrently running local processes on
this host. Each stdout and stderr stream is independently bounded by the
proof-wire process limit. Per-file and aggregate data limits are available on
the Python API; the CLI currently uses the conservative compiled defaults.

The output receipt is content addressed and atomically replaced, but it is not
a signature or an immutable CAS entry. Receipt validation defaults to rerunning
the complete official pipeline from the original formula and proof root.
F440 deliberately has no query-service environment variables yet: the current
SymCC/CaDiCaL producer does not emit a complete native PalRUP proof root.
Configuring this checker therefore does not enable proof generation.

The current result is a shared-filesystem checker mechanism. It is not a
multi-node transport or recovery implementation and does not establish solver
speedup, fuzzing coverage, defect yield or checker scalability. See
``codex/research-progress/PalRUP_Global_Confirmation_Pipeline_F440_2026-08-18.md``.

F441 generation-fenced MPI / ULFM recovery
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

F441 is an opt-in, independently testable recovery substrate. Build the pinned
Open MPI 5.0.10 runtime with::

  bash benchmark/install_openmpi_ulfm_5_0_10.sh

The default prefix is ``$HOME/.local/opt/openmpi-5.0.10-ulfm``. Override it
with ``PREFIX=/absolute/path``. The source URL and SHA-256 are fixed inside the
script. The required configure options are ``--with-ft=ulfm`` and
``--enable-mpi-ext=ftmpi``; the latter is the Open MPI public FT extension name.
The installer verifies ``mpi_ft_enable`` and the required ``MPIX_Comm_*``
symbols before reporting success.

Run the complete local gate with::

  F441_MPI_PREFIX="$HOME/.local/opt/openmpi-5.0.10-ulfm" \
  F441_OUTPUT_DIR=/tmp/f441 benchmark/run_f441_ulfm_tests.sh

``F441_MPI_PREFIX`` selects the qualified installation and
``F441_OUTPUT_DIR`` selects the artifact directory. The script supplies
``mpirun --with-ft ulfm`` and isolates ``PATH``/``LD_LIBRARY_PATH``. The local
physical-failure gate additionally uses TCP transport because the shared-memory
transport did not converge inside the test deadline on this host.

F443 production hot-path integration
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

F443 connects the F441 substrate to ``mpi_concolic_execution.py``. It remains
off by default. Enable it only under the pinned runtime and pass the FT launch
option::

  PATH="$HOME/.local/opt/openmpi-5.0.10-ulfm/bin:$PATH" \
  LD_LIBRARY_PATH="$HOME/.local/opt/openmpi-5.0.10-ulfm/lib" \
  SYMCC_ULFM_HOT_PATH=1 \
  mpirun --with-ft ulfm --mca pml ob1 --mca btl tcp,self -n 4 \
    python3 util/mpi_concolic_execution.py -i seeds -o corpus -- TARGET @@

Every rank first exercises the ULFM methods on ``COMM_SELF`` and allgathers the
complete recovery-policy SHA. Any missing or semantically unavailable operation,
or any per-rank policy mismatch, aborts with status 74; the driver never falls
back to non-fault-tolerant MPI. F446 extends this path to one or more master
groups. A failed worker or master is removed from the global communicator; all
survivors recompute the transport layout from stable initial endpoint identities
and build fresh group/master communicators for the new generation.

Runtime settings are:

* ``SYMCC_ULFM_REPAIR_ATTEMPTS``: bounded shrink/attestation attempts, default 3;
* ``SYMCC_ULFM_COLLECTIVE_TIMEOUT``: attestation collective deadline, default
  30 seconds;
* ``SYMCC_ULFM_POLL_INTERVAL``: nonblocking collective request polling, default
  0.01 seconds;
* ``SYMCC_ULFM_FAILURE_POLL_INTERVAL``: bounded local failure-trigger polling
  interval, default 0.1 seconds. The durable failure set is not taken from the
  local ``Get_failed`` view: survivors first shrink, exchange stable endpoint
  identities and derive the common missing set;
* ``SYMCC_ULFM_WARM_SPARES``: number of pre-launched high-rank standby processes,
  default 0. It must leave at least three active processes. Standbys participate
  in global recovery but not normal scheduling. After shrink, the lowest
  surviving standby identities are promoted until the fixed active budget is
  restored or the standby pool is exhausted.
* ``SYMCC_ULFM_SNAPSHOT_FILE_MODE``: explicit non-executable octal mode for the
  atomic global recovery snapshot, default ``0600``. It must retain owner read
  and write bits. Multi-user shared-storage deployments may opt into ``0660``
  or ``0666`` only after applying their site access policy;
* ``SYMCC_SHARED_CORPUS_FILE_MODE``: explicit non-executable octal mode for
  public content-addressed corpus objects, default ``0600``. The publisher
  applies it with ``fchmod`` and ``fsync`` before publication. Cross-user
  replay commonly uses ``0640`` or ``0644`` under an appropriate directory
  policy.

Each master durably checkpoints dispatch, completion and cancellation in a
generation-specific QueryStore. Global membership prepare/commit receipts and
sealed role manifests live below
``.standalone-work-<epoch>/ulfm-query-store/global-membership``. Each work
envelope and result contains the exact generation/shard/lease fence. On failure,
all survivors execute revoke, shrink, stable-endpoint attestation and agree;
the repaired generation reopens the shared work WAL, redoes committing records
and reclaims all pre-commit leases with a new token before dispatch resumes.

``SYMCC_ULFM_TEST_FAIL_INITIAL_RANK`` is a test-only one-shot physical failure
injection at generation zero. ``SYMCC_ULFM_TEST_FAILURE_SCHEDULE`` is the F446
test-only continuous-failure form: ``0:2,1:3`` kills stable initial rank 2 in
generation zero and rank 3 in generation one. Generation and rank entries must
be unique nonnegative integers; at most 64 events are accepted. Neither variable
may be set in a production campaign. Run the F443 worker/master gates with::

  F443_OUTPUT_DIR=/tmp/f443 benchmark/run_f443_ulfm_hot_path_tests.sh

The expected launcher status is 86 because one rank is intentionally terminated;
success is established by the independently verified survivor artifact. The
current evidence is same-host TCP transport, not a multi-node MTTR, coverage or
throughput claim. See
``codex/research-progress/Production_ULFM_Hot_Path_Recovery_F443_2026-08-25.md``.

Run the F446 multi-master, original-root, warm-spare and two-consecutive-failure
gate with::

  F446_OUTPUT_DIR=/tmp/f446 benchmark/run_f446_elastic_ulfm_tests.sh

The gate independently verifies the global QueryStore receipt sequence, every
sealed generation layout, exact standby promotions, final group controllers,
shared WAL commit manifests, public corpus hashes and log/WAL statistics
agreement. The current checked artifact is same-host physical-process-failure
mechanism evidence. Multi-node and 8/32/128-worker performance conclusions
require separately identified experiments; this gate does not imply them.

F447 adds a separate controlled two-host gate with shrink-first stable-identity
discovery, per-master/per-generation stores, serialized WAL replay and explicit
snapshot/corpus modes::

  F447_HOST_SPEC='node-a:9,node-b:1' \
  F447_MINIMUM_HOSTS=2 F447_OUTPUT_DIR=/shared/f447 \
    benchmark/run_f447_multinode_ulfm_test.sh

The F447 gate may use site-specific NAT/transport and shared-storage shims. Its
oracle therefore proves multi-host mechanism behavior under the recorded test
topology, not native production-network performance. The separately sealed
8/32/128-worker protocol was stopped early: nine valid paired blocks show
throughput improvement but no endpoint-coverage improvement, and 128 workers
failed internally in 2/11 balanced trials. Treat this as target-specific
engineering evidence, not a confirmatory production scale limit.

F448 proof-prefix guided certified QF_BV partitioning
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

F448 converts one deterministic bit-blast plan into an arbitrary number of
exhaustive, pairwise-disjoint cubes. Checked realtime clause-activity receipts
rank split variables; the ranking is an efficiency heuristic only. An
independent verifier replays the exact proof records and ACKs, recomputes the
ranking, then replays every binary split and checks the final rational load sum
is exactly one.

Generate a partition from a QueryStore and a saved realtime solver result with::

  python3 util/symcc_qfbv_partition.py \
    --query-store /shared/queries --query-id QUERY_ID \
    --proof-store /shared/qfbv-incremental-proofs \
    --partition-store /shared/qfbv-partitions \
    --lifecycle-store /shared/qfbv-lifecycle \
    --activity-result result.json \
    --cubes 128 --max-depth 7 --backlog-per-cube 1 \
    --output partition-jobs.json

``result.json`` must expose ``backend_realtime_import_acks`` and
``backend_realtime_clause_activity_receipts`` from a solver configured with
``realtime_stream.track_clause_activity=true``. Every activity receipt must
have one matching checked ACK. If ``--activity-result`` is omitted, the tool
uses deterministic static input-variable order when ``--static-fallback`` is
enabled. ``--no-static-fallback`` fails if checked activity supplies too few
independent variables. ``--no-input-variables-only`` permits internal CNF
variables and iterates that domain lazily.

The principal bounds are 4,096 cubes, depth 64, 4,096 activity receipts and a
64 MiB certificate. ``--verify-digest SHA256`` loads a CAS object and repeats
the complete verification against the current Query IR and proof store.

The query service accepts::

  --qfbv-partition-store PATH

or ``SYMCC_QFBV_PARTITION_STORE``. When F429 lifecycle GC is enabled, the same
partition store must be available to startup inventory and deletion routing.
The registry uses the independent ``partition`` artifact kind. Every checked
certificate automatically depends on its ``sat-proof`` records; publication
fails if those dependencies are absent from the selected lifecycle registry.
The store persists the registry identity and rejects unmanaged or different-
registry access after adoption.

The CLI emits exact cube assumptions and a stable malleable job catalog. F448
alone is partition/certificate mechanism evidence; F449 below connects those
assumptions to the main query-service execution loop. See
``codex/research-progress/Proof_Prefix_Guided_Certified_Partitioning_F448_2026-08-25.md``.

F449 proof-aware certified partition execution
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

F449 is disabled by default. Enable automatic partition construction and cube
execution with::

  python3 util/symcc_query_service.py \
    --store /shared/queries \
    --portfolio /shared/cadical-portfolio.json \
    --qfbv-incremental-proof-store /shared/qfbv-proofs \
    --qfbv-partition-store /shared/qfbv-partitions \
    --qfbv-partition-execution-store /shared/qfbv-executions \
    --qfbv-partition-cubes 64 \
    --qfbv-partition-parallelism 16 \
    --qfbv-partition-max-attempts 3 \
    --qfbv-partition-cube-timeout-ms 30000 \
    --qfbv-partition-task-lease-ms 60000 \
    --jobs 4

``--qfbv-partition-cubes`` is zero by default and otherwise must be in
``[2,4096]``. Enabling it requires exactly one ``bitblast-cadical-qfbv``
portfolio entry and configured incremental-proof, partition and execution
stores. The service fails closed instead of silently executing uncertified
cubes. The portfolio may be one-shot or persistent; persistent mode keeps one
per-slot native context and reuses the permanent formula while installing cube
literals as solve-local assumptions.

``--qfbv-partition-parallelism`` is the total in-process cube-slot budget in
``[1,256]``. ``--jobs`` may not exceed it. Each outer query worker receives
``floor(partition_parallelism/jobs)`` independent backend slots, preventing
outer query parallelism from multiplying the configured solver budget.

``--qfbv-partition-max-attempts`` is in ``[1,32]``.
``--qfbv-partition-cube-timeout-ms`` is in ``[1,3600000]``.
``--qfbv-partition-task-lease-ms`` is in ``[100,86400000]`` and must be at
least the cube timeout. Active cube leases heartbeat at approximately one third
of the lease interval; an expired lease is reclaimed with a new token until the
attempt budget is exhausted.

The equivalent environment variables are::

  SYMCC_QFBV_PARTITION_CUBES
  SYMCC_QFBV_PARTITION_PARALLELISM
  SYMCC_QFBV_PARTITION_MAX_ATTEMPTS
  SYMCC_QFBV_PARTITION_CUBE_TIMEOUT_MS
  SYMCC_QFBV_PARTITION_TASK_LEASE_MS
  SYMCC_QFBV_PARTITION_EXECUTION_STORE

The parent QueryStore lease is renewed throughout the nested solve. SAT commits
only after cube-polarity and Query IR replay, then cooperatively cancels active
peers. UNSAT commits only after every signed assumption-scoped receipt is
checked and the certified split tree resolves to a base-query receipt.

With F429 lifecycle GC, the execution store uses the independent
``partition-execution`` kind. Terminal records depend on the partition and
proof closure; active records make inventory incomplete and block collection.
Startup GC therefore requires the same execution store and lifecycle registry
used during solving.

F449 establishes an end-to-end query-service mechanism, not a performance
claim. Its oracle measures Python/SQLite/proof-pipeline cost and does not replace
an equal-CPU public benchmark. See
``codex/research-progress/Proof_Aware_Certified_Partition_Execution_F449_2026-08-25.md``.

F450 closure-bound proof replay authorization cache
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

F450 is enabled whenever the incremental proof checker is configured. It
retains checked clause authorizations only inside one process and only for the
same canonical, immutable ``BitBlastPlan`` object. Configure both bounds with::

  --qfbv-proof-replay-cache-entries 4096
  --qfbv-proof-replay-cache-bytes 16777216

or::

  SYMCC_QFBV_PROOF_REPLAY_CACHE_ENTRIES=4096
  SYMCC_QFBV_PROOF_REPLAY_CACHE_BYTES=16777216

The entry limit is in ``[0,1000000]`` and the accounted-byte limit is in
``[0,17179869184]``. Both values must be zero to disable the cache; disabling
only one fails at startup. The default is 4,096 entries and 16 MiB.

A cache hit does not trust a stale proof object. The checker first normalizes
and re-identifies the root record, then stable-reads every object in the cached
transitive import closure under one proof-store lock and verifies canonical
path, indexed size and encoded SHA-256. Only a complete successful attestation
increments ``hits``. A missing or changed dependency evicts the exact entry and
fails closed. Noncanonical manually mutable plans replay normally but are never
cached.

Live and final service statistics expose ``qfbv_proof_replay_cache`` with the
policy digest, both budgets, current entries/accounted bytes, hits, misses,
inserts, evictions, oversized entries, bypasses, attestation failures, and
attested object/byte totals. The cache has an independent policy identity and
does not alter the persistent proof checker or receipt identity.

F450 is a proof-DAG replay optimization, not a persistent trust source or a SAT
result cache. See
``codex/research-progress/Closure_Bound_Proof_Replay_Cache_F450_2026-08-25.md``.

F451 generation-fenced distributed certified cube execution
-------------------------------------------------------------

``util/symcc_qfbv_distributed_partition.py`` exposes a restart-safe command
surface over the existing QueryStore, F448 partition CAS, F449 execution
ledger, F451 binding ledger, and F447 durable ULFM snapshot::

  python3 util/symcc_qfbv_distributed_partition.py init ...
  python3 util/symcc_qfbv_distributed_partition.py claim ...
  python3 util/symcc_qfbv_distributed_partition.py heartbeat ...
  python3 util/symcc_qfbv_distributed_partition.py complete ...
  python3 util/symcc_qfbv_distributed_partition.py recover ...
  python3 util/symcc_qfbv_distributed_partition.py reconcile ...
  python3 util/symcc_qfbv_distributed_partition.py finalize ...
  python3 util/symcc_qfbv_distributed_partition.py stats ...

Every command receives explicit artifact/store paths and reconstructs current
state; no Python coordinator process is a trust root. ``claim`` emits schema
``symcc-qfbv-generation-fenced-cube-lease-v1``. The canonical lease binds the
F449 execution/cube/token to ``run_id=qfbv-cubes:<execution_sha256>``, the exact
``work_id=cube:<execution_sha256>:<ordinal>``, stable endpoint, generation,
