shard token, work token, and both expiry policies.

``heartbeat`` and ``complete`` require both leases to be current. SAT completion
requires ``--input-hex`` and revalidates the candidate against the parent Query
IR; UNSAT completion replays the assumption-scoped proof. ``recover`` first
commits the new ULFM generation and then re-fences all ambiguous in-flight
cubes. ``reconcile`` resumes a committed recovery queue without advancing
another generation and repairs the two recognized cross-ledger crash windows.

The binding database uses a stable no-follow initialization lock, SQLite
``synchronous=FULL`` and unique inner/outer lease constraints. It is an audit
and reconciliation ledger, not a replacement SAT/UNSAT checker. F451 has no new
``SYMCC_*`` environment setting; deployment policy stays explicit at the CLI
boundary. See
``codex/research-progress/Generation_Fenced_Distributed_Cube_Execution_F451_2026-08-26.md``.

F452 native checker clause compression
----------------------------------------

F452 is built into the CaDiCaL realtime shim by
``benchmark/install_cadical_3_0_1.sh``. The installer compiles
``qfbv_cadical_realtime.cpp`` and ``qfbv_clause_compression.cpp`` into one
shared object and rejects missing protocol, encode, or decode symbols.

Existing realtime shims remain compatible by default. A persistent
``bitblast-cadical-qfbv`` portfolio entry can require the F452 capability::

  {
    "realtime_stream": {
      "library": "/opt/cadical/lib/libsymcc_qfbv_cadical_realtime.so",
      "require_clause_compression": true
    }
  }

``require_clause_compression`` must be a JSON boolean and defaults to false.
When true, startup requires exact protocol
``symcc-qfbv-native-clause-compression-v1``; a missing, partial, non-ASCII, or
unknown ABI fails closed. Compression does not change
``native_queue_literals``: that limit remains a semantic literal-count budget,
while native telemetry separately reports encoded queue bytes.

The shared object also exports a two-call C ABI for diagnostics and independent
oracles: ``symcc_qfbv_clause_compress`` and
``symcc_qfbv_clause_decompress``. Call first with zero capacity to obtain the
required size, then with an exact or larger buffer. Insufficient capacity does
not partially write. See
``codex/research-progress/Native_Checker_Clause_Compression_F452_2026-08-26.md``.

F453 online activity/cost-guided QF_BV cubing
----------------------------------------------

F453 is disabled by default and requires F449 partition execution. Enable it
with ``--qfbv-online-cubing`` or ``SYMCC_QFBV_ONLINE_CUBING=1``. The persistent
policy database defaults below QueryStore; production deployments should use
an explicit stable path with ``--qfbv-online-cubing-store PATH`` or
``SYMCC_QFBV_ONLINE_CUBING_STORE``.

The command-line policy controls are::

  --qfbv-online-cubing-candidates 2,4,8,16
  --qfbv-online-cubing-strategies static,activity,cost
  --qfbv-online-cubing-prerun-ms 250
  --qfbv-online-cubing-min-exploration 2
  --qfbv-online-cubing-cost-min-exploration 1
  --qfbv-online-cubing-exploration-permille 100
  --qfbv-online-cubing-cost-reference-us 1000000

Their environment equivalents are
``SYMCC_QFBV_ONLINE_CUBING_CANDIDATES``,
``SYMCC_QFBV_ONLINE_CUBING_STRATEGIES``,
``SYMCC_QFBV_ONLINE_CUBING_PRERUN_MS``,
``SYMCC_QFBV_ONLINE_CUBING_MIN_EXPLORATION``,
``SYMCC_QFBV_ONLINE_CUBING_COST_MIN_EXPLORATION``,
``SYMCC_QFBV_ONLINE_CUBING_EXPLORATION_PERMILLE`` and
``SYMCC_QFBV_ONLINE_CUBING_COST_REFERENCE_US``. Lists must be comma-separated
canonical values. ``static`` is mandatory and must be first. Candidate counts
must be strictly increasing, include the configured base cube count and remain
in ``[2,4096]``.

``activity`` and ``cost`` require a persistent realtime CaDiCaL portfolio
entry with clause-activity tracking. Their bounded prerun consumes only
ACK-paired checked activity; lack of capability makes the request fall back to
``static``. A verified prerun SAT model or authorized UNSAT proof may terminate
the query. Every other result continues through F448 and F449.

All arms share the same configured CPU-ms ceiling. Guided prerun is deducted
from the ceiling and the remainder is divided across the selected cube count
and F449 maximum attempts. The policy database is identity-bound: changing any
policy field requires a new store path. See
``codex/research-progress/Online_Activity_Cost_Guided_Cubing_F453_2026-08-26.md``.

F454 solver-native PalRUP production
-------------------------------------

F454 is an explicit proof-production command, not a default query-service
switch. Install the fixed SAT 2026 producer fork and wrapper libraries with::

  benchmark/install_cadical_palrup_sat2026.sh

The default prefix is
``$HOME/.local/opt/cadical-palrup-sat2026``. Override it with
``CADICAL_PALRUP_PREFIX``; override the source checkout with
``CADICAL_PALRUP_SOURCE``. The installer requires the exact upstream remote and
commit, rejects dirty tracked source, compiles both wrappers with
``-Wall -Wextra -Werror``, checks the exported C ABI, and records source and
binary hashes under ``share/``.

The production CLI requires an input DIMACS file, a new proof-root path, a
solver count, the native pool library and helper, all three official PalRUP
tools, and the exact SHA-256 of every executable input. Operational limits are::

  --producer-timeout-ms 300000
  --checker-timeout-ms 300000
  --max-parallel 16
  --maximum-shared-clause-length 32
  --queue-capacity-clauses 65536

``max-parallel`` bounds the actual number of native solver ranks. The shared
clause length and queue capacity affect performance only: queue backpressure
may drop clauses, but every published UNSAT root must still pass the official
``local_check -> redistribute -> confirm`` pipeline. The proof-root path must
not already exist; publication uses no-replace atomic rename and a published
tree is promoted only after the complete staging tree passes an independent
receipt and official-proof recheck. The acceptance oracle additionally rechecks
the final published path.

F454 pins the Mallob CaDiCaL fork commit
``be7a0f84190b3216c589696b2010e8cbf8a8252e`` and the PalRUP checker commit
``d9382fb4b0acf094034ee91e2ed0a22b1b479c1d``. See
``codex/research-progress/Solver_Native_Clause_Sharing_PalRUP_Production_F454_2026-08-26.md``.


F456 POSE-C initial symbolic heap
---------------------------------

F456 is an explicit API capability and adds no implicit ``SYMCC_*`` switch.
Create a ``PoseHeapState`` with bounded ``PoseTypeLayout`` values and named
pointer roots, then use ``load``, ``load_reference``, ``store``, ``free`` and
``branch_alias`` from ``util/pose_symbolic_heap.py``. Heap refinement preserves
one state; only ``branch_alias`` denotes a real CFG decision and may create
children. ``to_smt2`` emits deterministic QF_BV, while ``validate_model`` and
``materialize_model`` enforce the solver-to-concrete trust boundary.

The fixed admission limits are 128 layouts, 256 references, 256 proxy objects,
131072 term nodes, 1048576 byte cells and 65536 conditions. Type size is at
most 1 MiB. Object offsets and widths are concrete and bounds checked;
reference fields are 64-bit little-endian words. ``persist_pose_heap`` attaches
the canonical state to a live continuation under ``@pose:initial-heap`` and
``restore_pose_heap`` validates both the outer expression root and inner state
digest before returning it.

The caller must provide layouts, roots and heap operations. Arbitrary symbolic
pointer arithmetic, automatic LLVM debug-type recovery, union/type punning,
custom allocator semantics and concurrent-heap linearizability are outside
this contract. See
``codex/research-progress/POSE_C_Initial_Symbolic_Heap_F456_2026-08-26.md``.


F463 failure-atomic control-plane closure
------------------------------------------

F463 adds no new ``SYMCC_*`` variable. It tightens the behavior of existing
interfaces. ``AflConfig`` parses the ``command_line`` field with shell quoting,
replaces ``@@`` wherever it occurs inside a target argument, and rejects an
empty command or a ``--`` delimiter without a target. The AFL coverage bridge
retries failed stable queue objects on every normal coverage poll instead of
waiting for the periodic directory audit.

The asynchronous query service runs in a dedicated process session. Master
shutdown sends TERM and, after a bounded wait, KILL to that entire process
group so persistent solver descendants share the service cleanup boundary.
Runtime service exit closes its log and disables QueryStore-dependent semantic
proposals; already published candidate files remain eligible for scanning.

QueryStore terminal worker failure now commits query ``done`` state, the error
result, and its publication outbox row in one SQLite transaction. Concurrent
publication failures increment the authoritative retry counter under a write
lock. Parallel-scale CSV integer counts are parsed exactly rather than through
binary floating point, bounded to ``uint64``, and a model horizon is no longer
treated as evidence of saturation. Non-finite conversions and overflowing
weighted products are rejected before fitting. See
``codex/research-progress/Third_Deep_Review_and_Failure_Atomic_Closure_F463_2026-08-31.md``.


F464 semantic, transport, and scaling closure
----------------------------------------------

F464 corrects GEP symbolic arithmetic for targets whose DataLayout pointer
index width differs from either the source index or pointer width. Non-struct
indexes are sign-extended or truncated to the address-space index width,
scaled by allocation size, and accumulated with index-width wrapping. When the
pointer is wider, only its low index-width slice changes; high pointer bits are
preserved. Structure-member allocation offsets remain 64-bit throughout the
lowering, including layouts beyond 4 GiB. Scalable-vector allocation uses the concrete execution's
``llvm.vscale``. Unsupported widths and non-integral pointer address spaces fail
closed to concretization.
There is no new switch.

``SYMCC_RESULT_OBJECT_TRANSPORT=0/1`` (default 1) controls result-side CAS
transport in ``mpi_fuzzing_helper.py``. A worker publishes each admitted child
under ``<symcc-output>/.result_objects/<sha256-prefix>/<sha256-rest>`` and sends
only ``object_id`` plus ``object_size`` in the MPI RESULT. The Master performs
a stable digest/identity check during bounded asynchronous admission and again
when one candidate is materialized for triage. A clean all-worker STOP/ACK
removes the transport-only store. A transient publication error retains the
already bounded inline-byte form; disabling the option always uses that legacy
form. This removes the normal
``admission_capacity * per-result-byte-budget`` retained-byte amplification,
but it does not bound mpi4py's initial pickle receive allocation or a deliberate
inline fallback.

Worker-side coverage filtering transmits only the bitmap bits first introduced
by each candidate, rather than repeating its complete sparse edge set. Candidate
rows and the optional proposal bitmap in one RESULT share an aggregate
``StreamingShowmap.MAX_EDGES`` bound; overflow rejects the whole parent and
reports ``coverage_rows`` budget telemetry. Worker coverage and cross-item
content dedup are committed only after the complete parent fits that budget.
Master still merges candidates in their original order, so this is an exact
union reduction rather than coverage sampling.

The AFL coverage bridge uses the following bounded controls:

- ``SYMCC_AFL_COVERAGE_JOBS`` (default 1, range 1--16) and
  ``SYMCC_AFL_COVERAGE_PENDING`` (default 256, range 1--65536) bound concurrent
  showmap work and in-flight entries.
- ``SYMCC_AFL_COVERAGE_RETRIES`` (default 65536, range 1--1000000) is the
  maximum number selected from the durable retry ledger in one scheduling
  batch. It is not a retention cap and never evicts failed observations.
- ``SYMCC_AFL_COVERAGE_RETRY_BASE_SEC`` (default 0.1, range 0--3600) and
  ``SYMCC_AFL_COVERAGE_RETRY_MAX_SEC`` (default 30, range BASE--86400) control
  exponential retry delay. The single-slot scheduler alternates new queue work
  and due retries; larger pools reserve at least one quarter of available
  capacity for due retries without leaving unused capacity.

``libafl_data_coverage_rt.so`` now requires pthread linkage. Its first-use
initialization uses ``pthread_once`` and re-entrant raw comparison loops, and
native map-byte increments are atomic and never leave AFL's reserved zero
value. This makes concurrent first comparison deterministic without changing
the existing ``SYMCC_AFL_DATA_COVERAGE`` or ``AFL_DATA_COVERAGE`` switches.

Hybrid benchmark CSV schema adds ``auxiliary_compute_slots``. The Master emits
the configured concurrency used by the query, result-admission, coverage,
density, and auxiliary-showmap pools; ``run_benchmark.py`` preserves the value.
Parallel-scale analysis schema v6 subtracts it, together with coordinators,
from physical cores. The ledger covers successful, failed, and timed-out
attempts; missing or scale-varying hybrid values fail the decision-quality
gate. Repeated observations are reduced to exact sufficient statistics while
retaining within-level squared error, including the fitted baseline anchor, in
R-squared and RMSE; paired bootstrap fits cache identical seed-count vectors
without changing the requested replicate distribution.

Import-time ``SYMCC_TIMEOUT`` and ``SYMCC_MAX_DEPTH`` parsing is total and
bounded. An unexpected Master exception now prints a traceback, attempts the
same bounded cooperative STOP/ACK cleanup as ordinary shutdown, and then
returns a non-zero MPI status. A coordinator with no worker, an existing output
directory without explicit resume, and a queue-lock ownership conflict also
remain configuration failures even if every worker acknowledges shutdown. See
``codex/research-progress/Fourth_Deep_Review_Semantic_Transport_Scaling_F464_2026-09-03.md``.

(Most people should stop reading here.)


                                Advanced options


There is actually a third category of options: when compiling with SymCC, you
can specify the location of its various components via environment variables.
This is not necessary in most cases because the build system makes sure that all
components know about each other; however, in some advanced setups you may need
to move files around after building them, and in that case, you can use the
variables documented below to communicate the new locations:

- SYMCC_RUNTIME_DIR and SYMCC_RUNTIME32_DIR: The directory that contains the
  run-time support library (i.e., libSymRuntime.so).

- SYMCC_PASS_DIR: The directory containing the compiler pass (i.e.,
  libSymbolize.so).

- SYMCC_CLANG and SYMCC_CLANGPP: The clang and clang++ binaries to use during
  compilation. Be very careful with this one: if the version of the compiler you
  specify here doesn't match the one you built SymCC against, you'll most likely
  get linker errors.
